# Behaviour: what the replicator does, case by case

Authored by Claude (claude-fable-5-2).

Version 3. What changed from version 2 is listed at the end of section 7.

**Scope, stated plainly.** Sections 3 to 5 account for every circumstance the code distinguishes: every variant of its closed enums has a row. That is not every circumstance the world produces. Circumstances the code does not distinguish (a paused process, two processes on one staging directory, a volume snapshot put back) are in section 2 and in the `WORLD` rows of section 3, most of them marked untested or unknown. The list of circumstances is open and will stay open; section 7 says what would shrink it.

**Read at** `origin/main` 50fb763; the rows #104 changed were read again at dd1d89b, and UP-14, WORLD-5, WORLD-32 and GO-9 at fdf1920db. Paths are under `crates/` unless they start with `docs/` or `scripts/`. Nothing was built or run to write it: every statement is a reading of the code, of a named test, or of a named report. Code is cited by file and symbol, never by line. Everything cited is a path in the repository, a test name, a commit, or a pull request or issue number on emschwartz/litestream-rs. Two measurements from earlier drafts were cut because their reports are not in the repository.

**Evidence marks.** A row's Mark column says what a test has shown, not what the row claims. Three marks can be earned, and they are separate:

| Mark | Earned when |
|---|---|
| **D** detected | A named test produces the circumstance, or injects its error, and asserts the response and the signal the row states. Says nothing about any restore |
| **S** survived | A restore is judged correct while the circumstance still holds |
| **R** recovered | A restore is judged correct after the circumstance cleared, or after the engine's one-time decision (a snapshot, a continue) took effect |

A row marked "D R" has no evidence about restores made during the fault. For a circumstance that is a single decision and not a lasting condition (most boot rows) S has no meaning and its absence is not a gap. For a lasting condition (credentials revoked, a full disk, a second writer) the absence of S is a gap: almost every simulator scenario injects one error at one call and judges the restore only after recovery (`injected` in `sim/src/scenario/faults.rs`). The random swarm does restore at arbitrary moments, including while rate faults are active, but no row can cite a swarm run as its evidence, so none earns S that way. Lasting faults now have a rig: a hold (`Op::Hold`, `sim/src/run/hold.rs`) keeps a fault in place for 2 to 11 ticks while the app commits, judges the raw replica with both judges in the middle and at the end, and restores through the fault where a restore meets it. The written histories in `sim/tests/sim/survival.rs` are what the S marks cite. A hold of WAL damage is judged against a model that adopted the damage, so it earns no S for the WAL rows.

Rows that earn none of the three carry one of these instead:

| Mark | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **code only** | Read in the code. No test asserts it |
| **assumed** | Taken from outside the project and not observed here |
| **untested** | Believed from reading; nothing exercises the circumstance |
| **unknown** | Nobody knows what happens |
| **known wrong** | A defect is open. Section 6 has it |
| **accepted, undetected** | A stated limit of the design |

Evidence is cited as: a test name with its crate ("sim" is `sim/tests/sim`, "core" is a unit test in `core/src`, "compat", "sqlite", "history", "cli", "replica", "config" likewise); oracle `X`, a violation the simulator's judge raises on any seed (`Violation` in `sim/src/oracle.rs`); judge `X`, an anomaly of the history judge, which reads only restored content and a commit log (`Anomaly` in `history/src/judge.rs`); plant `X`, a defect kept as a patch in `docs/planted-bugs/` and re-planted nightly.

The count of rows by mark is not kept here, since a count in a committed file goes stale when two branches each add a row: `just behavior-tally` prints it from `docs/behavior.toml`.

**Changes in review.** None. Main is described as it is. Four pull requests were once listed here: #96 (`claude/listing-trust`) and #89 (`claude/rewound-snapshot`) were closed unmerged, and #86 and #97 merged. A row whose In review cell still names one of them has a stale tag and is read again when it is next touched.

---

## 1. Guarantees

Each guarantee is stated as something an outside observer can check, the conditions it needs, the rows that count against it, and its evidence with seeds and sizes. G0 and G7 are liveness and health properties: without them an engine that uploads nothing would satisfy the rest.

| Guarantee | Open on main | Counted against it |
|---|---|---|
| G0 a restore of the latest state succeeds and is not older than what health reported | Yes | UP-11 (unseen case), MAINT-11, D2 (accepted) |
| G1 a successful restore is a committed state | Hazards, none with a test that fails on main in the gate | BOOT-19 (accepted, as Go does it, D173), BOOT-18 (shown on #96's tree only), RUN-12 under the default response |
| G2 latest does not go backwards unreported | None shown on main | The exceptions' signals can be missing after a bucket rollback (D17, CHK-4) |
| G3 as-of returns nothing begun after the instant | None since #98 | Checked by the simulator's per-read clock since C7 was fixed (RUN-25, RUN-26) |
| G4 lag | No tested bound | N1 |
| G7 health is not clean while replication is halted | Yes | N1 |

Version 0.0.0: these are what the code is built to deliver.

### Assumptions

| # | Assumption | State on main | What happens when it is false |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | An fsync that returns success made the bytes durable | Assumed | A power loss can rewind the database below the replica. Detected in part (BOOT-9); see G2 |
| A2 | SQLite's file locks and shared memory work on this filesystem | Assumed. No self-test | The read-lock proofs (RUN-7, CKPT-1) prove nothing. **unknown** on NFS, virtiofs, gRPC-FUSE |
| A3 | Only SQLite writes the database file and the WAL | Partly checked | A foreign write to the WAL is caught by frame checksums (RUN-4). A foreign write to a database page is caught only if the engine has seen that page before (RUN-12) |
| A4 | One replicator process writes to a replica prefix, and one process uses a staging directory | Staging: enforced for engines of this port on one host with the meta directory on a local filesystem, by an exclusive lock on `<meta>/lsr-lock` (CFG-36, WORLD-1), which the library refuses at once and `lsr replicate` waits for; not across NFS, SMB or FUSE mounts, and not against a Go process. Prefix: not enforced | A second writer on the prefix is detected from a listing, late (UP-10, UP-14), and at boot whenever its files are above this database's position and neither the live WAL nor a takeover record vouches for them (BOOT-22). Two processes on one staging directory outside the lock's reach are not detected (WORLD-1) |
| A5 | The store returns the bytes it acknowledged, or bytes that fail to decode | Checked per file | The SHA-256 of every body signed into its request and checked by the store, and a full LTX decode on every read. A different valid file under the same key is not caught (L1) |
| A6 | Every listing shows every object the bucket holds. This covers the engine's listings and the one listing a restore makes | **Unsupported where false**: a store whose listings lag is outside the supported set (D173), and the consistency probe (release bar R9) is the gate | Engine side: a transaction id can be reused for other content (BOOT-18). Restore side: an older state can be returned as "latest" with exit 0 (WORLD-4) |
| A7 | The bucket is never replaced by an older copy of itself while the engine is down | **Not detected** (a documented limit, D173) | The older bucket is taken as the bucket (BOOT-19) |
| A8 | The database, WAL and staging directory are never together replaced by an older copy (a VM or volume snapshot put back) | Detected: the boot refuses the replica's newer files (`other_writer`, BOOT-22) until an operator runs `lsr trust-local` (this copy is the right one) or `lsr restore` (the replica is); not prevented | None until the operator chooses. After `lsr trust-local` the latest state goes back to the snapshot's state (WORLD-3), a G2 exception the operator asked for |
| A9 | 64-bit checksums do not collide; a WAL `salt2` (32 random bits) does not repeat across the restarts being compared | Accepted risk | Not testable |
| A10 | The application runs `synchronous=NORMAL` or `FULL` | Not checked | Under `OFF` SQLite does not sync before a checkpoint and the engine's fsync of the WAL is the only barrier. **unknown** |

### G1. A successful restore is a state the application committed

**Statement.** Let `restore` return success for a target (latest, a transaction id, an instant). The published database is page for page the database as some commit left it. Conditions the operator owes: A3 (only SQLite writes the files), A4 (one writer), A5 (the store returns what it acknowledged). A6 and A7 are **not** conditions: a listing that lags and a bucket that was rolled back are faults the engine is meant to survive, and where it does not, that counts against G1 (BOOT-18, BOOT-19).

**What makes a violation silent.** When the replica holds a chain of files that each verify and together are wrong, `restore` cannot tell (REST-9): it returns exit 0. So G1 is kept or lost by the engine at upload time, never recovered at restore time.

**Counted against G1.**
- BOOT-19: across a restart after a bucket rollback, one transaction id is put twice with different bytes. Not defended: Go does not notice a rollback either, and the next snapshot makes the replica whole (`docs/decisions/D173.md`). No restore has been shown wrong from it.
- RUN-12: under the default response a page no commit wrote is shipped. Deliberate, reported while it lasts, and still a restore that is not a committed state.
- WORLD-1 is suspected, not shown.

**Qualifiers.**
- A database-file page is trusted on its first read after the page table was emptied (`core/src/pages.rs`, "What it cannot check").
- "Committed" includes commits the local database later lost to a lying disk (G2).

**Engine-level evidence.**

| Evidence | Size and fault mix | Runs |
|---|---|---|
| Simulator fixed seeds: sim `seeds_1_to_8` through `seeds_57_to_64`, each judged by oracle `RestoreMismatch`, `WrongAnswer`, `RestoreRefused`, `CoversMidTx`, `UnstagedPut` and by the history judge through oracle `History(NotInHistory, Broken)` | 64 seeds of the default mix: commits, crashes, power loss (prefix law), store faults, clock jumps, staging damage, second writer, rollback. No lagging listings | Every commit (the gate) |
| Simulator regression seeds in `sim/tests/sim/fixed_seeds.rs` (about 30 named) | One history each | The gate |
| Simulator rotating seeds: sim `soak` | Default and swarm mixes for a time budget (`just sim-nightly`, 900 s) | Nightly. `#[ignore]` in the gate |
| Real engine, directory store: history `every_restore_of_a_file_replica_is_a_node_of_the_history` | **One seed (7), 60 steps, no faults, clean drained stops only** | The gate |
| Real engine, MinIO: history `every_restore_of_a_minio_replica_is_a_node_of_the_history` | The same driver against MinIO | **`#[ignore]`**. Run by `just test-s3`, which CI runs on pull requests |
| Real SQLite under the block crash law: history `every_real_restore_through_power_losses_is_a_node_of_the_history` | **One seed, 4 cycles** | The gate. The 50-seed version, `real_power_loss_trials`, is **`#[ignore]`** and runs nightly (`just power-loss-trials`, `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`) |
| Real SQLite crash cycles: sqlite `crash_cycles_under_the_block_law_restore_committed_states` | **3 frozen seeds** | The gate. `crash_cycle_campaign` is **`#[ignore]`** and runs nightly in the same recipe |
| Real binary on LazyFS: `scripts/lazyfs.py` | 300 cycles nightly; latest state only. The README on `claude/readme` reports 1000 cycles with 0 anomalies (at most about 0.3% per cycle at 95%, that fault mix only). Not re-run here | Nightly |

**Blind spots in that evidence.** Oracle `TxidReused` and the increasing-cover check used to be switched off for a whole run once any rewind, bucket rollback or lost file had occurred. They are now scoped (CHK-1): an id may cover a second commit only if the replica lost the first from outside, or a power loss cut the first before the second was put. Section 6, C1.

### G2. The latest state does not go backwards, except after a reported loss

**Statement.** Take two successful restores of the latest state, the second after the first, with A4 holding. The second is the same commit or a descendant, unless one of the events below happened between them.

| Exception | The engine's signal | Can the signal be missing? |
|---|---|---|
| The local database rewound below what the replica held: power loss on a disk that lied about fsync, a WAL cut by another program, or routine loss under a failed barrier | Snapshot cause `local_rewound`; `RecoverEvent::LocalRewound` | Yes, when the regrown WAL is byte-identical in the staged range. Then nothing differs either |
| Database, WAL and staging were all put back from an older copy (VM or volume snapshot; WORLD-3) | Stop `other_writer`, with the error line "replicate refused: the replica holds transactions up to {txid} ..." (`Event::Refused`) | No: another writer's files at boot give the same stop (BOOT-22). The line names both ways out. A wiped staging directory no longer raises it when the WAL vouches for the replica (BOOT-6) |
| The WAL was lost and the database file is older than the replica (an operator restored the file; a WAL deleted before its frames were checkpointed) | Snapshot cause `wal_missing` or `generation_changed` | No, but these causes are also routine and harmless, so they do not identify a regression |
| Something removed the replica's newest files while the engine ran | Degraded cause `remote_regressed` when a listing ends below what the engine confirmed (UP-11): the uploader's, at boot and after an ambiguous commit, or maintenance's. A hole below the newest file is not watched, and restore reports it | Yes (D17) |
| The restore's own listing lagged (WORLD-4) | None. The engine is not involved | Always |

After a rewind the replica still holds the lost branch. Its transactions stay restorable by id or instant until retention removes them.

**Counted against G2.** Nothing shown on main. Three simulator seeds once showed the latest state going back after a rollback and a restored staging copy; they no longer arise since the engine stopped taking a staging copy put back from another timeline as a cost to defend against (D173, D175: a copy put back is uploaded as staged, which accepted cost (1) names). What remains from that family is id reuse, a G1 hazard (BOOT-19). G2 stays falsifiable only by the driver that injected the fault, because in two rows of the table above the engine's signal can be missing (C3).

**Engine-level evidence.** The same runs as G1, judged by judge `WentBackwards` (through oracle `History`) and oracle `LatestLostAck`.

**What that evidence does not show.**
- That every exception was reported. The simulator's history judge now requires the engine's own loss report before a recorded rewind explains a regression (CHK-3). A regression the driver excused (a bucket rollback) is a debt the engine must pay with a report later; some are never paid, which is D17 (only a listing that ends below what the engine confirmed raises `remote_regressed`). That is counted, not fatal (CHK-4). The real-engine history rig does not require the signal.
- `LatestLostAck` keeps acknowledgements across power loss now (CHK-2); a rollback voids those from the lowest id it uncovered.
- v1 cited history `a_recorded_rewind_explains_going_back_and_the_lost_branch_is_ahead` and `a_rewind_explains_nothing_below_its_target` here. Those feed a hand-built log to the judge. They show the checker can say no. They are not evidence about the engine.

### G3. A restore as of an instant contains no commit that began after the instant

**Statement.** A successful `restore` as of instant `t` contains no commit that began after `t` by the host's clock. This is the form the history judge checks since commit 938dbe7. The mechanism: the planner applies only files recorded as created strictly before `t`; a level-0 file is stamped after the committed read that bounds it (#98), so its stamp is not earlier than any commit it carries; a merged file carries its newest input's time. **G3 is an upper bound only.** Any older state satisfies it. How old the answer may be is governed by lag (G4), by outages, and by retention.

**Counted against G3.** Nothing on main. Until #98 the file was stamped with a clock reading taken before the committed read, and the history rig once saw a state committed after the instant (issue #99). Guarded now by core `a_commit_after_the_tick_began_is_not_stamped_before_it_landed`, `a_commit_before_the_checkpoint_barrier_is_not_stamped_before_it_landed` and plants `stamp-before-committed-read`, `seal-stamped-at-checkpoint-start`.

**Not bounded.**
- A commit is dated by when it was staged. Commits made while the engine was down or degraded are stamped at catch-up, so a restore as of an instant inside the outage leaves them out (D10). Bound: the outage. **untested**.
- An instant inside a merged file whose finer inputs are gone refuses with `AsOfInsideFile`; `--allow-earlier` returns the newest state provably before it.
- The times are the host's wall clock, never below its highest reading within one process (RUN-19), not across a restart (WORLD-13). A forward jump is believed; restores as of instants in that window are off by up to the jump. **untested**.

**Engine-level evidence.** oracle `AsOfTooNew`, `NotLatest`, `WrongCreatedAt` on the simulator runs listed under G1; sim `as_of_never_takes_an_upload_time_for_a_commit_time`; plant `time-provenance`. The history rig restores at two instants per judged moment and applies judge `AsOfTooNew`.

**What that evidence does not show.** Under its per-read clock law the simulator reaches the window #98 fixed and kills the re-planted defect unaided (RUN-25, RUN-26; C7). v1 cited history `as_of_is_never_newer_than_the_instant` and `as_of_within_a_bound_is_the_node_live_then`; those test the judge on a hand-built log, not the engine.

### G4. Lag

**Design, not a tested bound.** With every task healthy, the store reachable, and writes slower than the upload path, a commit is fsynced and staged within one poll interval (1 s by default) and uploaded on the following ticks. No test asserts a wall-clock bound. **untested.**

**Tested.**
- After a stop that reports `Stopped::Drained { txid }`, a restore of the latest state is the local head. Evidence: the history rig applies judge `Lagging` with bound 0 at drained stops (one seed, see G1); core `drain_finishes_uploads`.
- A stop whose deadline passes reports what was left, counted high: core `drain_deadline_stops`, `a_zero_deadline_reports_unsynced_bytes_and_does_no_work`.

**What is lost if the host is destroyed.** Every commit after `replicated_txid`.

**How to see lag.** `lsr_unreplicated_age_seconds` is the intended measure. Until a booted engine shows the replica caught up (before a process's first boot, and while a rebuilt engine waits to boot) it counts from the start of that wait, never 0 (replica `the_lag_counts_from_the_start_while_the_first_boot_fails`). It still restarts from the process's start after every restart, so a crash loop understates lag (N1); alert also on `lsr_replicated_txid` failing to advance while the application writes.

**When degraded or retrying.** Lag is unbounded. The staging directory grows when uploads fail; the WAL grows when sync fails. No bound is enforced on either. **code only.**

### G0. A restore of the latest state succeeds, and is not older than what health reported

**Statement.** At any moment after the first upload, `restore` of the latest state succeeds, and the state it returns contains every commit up to the `replicated_txid` that health last reported. Exceptions: the G2 exceptions, and a store that is unreachable to the restore itself.

**Counted against G0.**
- UP-11 (a hole below the newest file) and MAINT-11: after files are removed from the replica while the engine runs, a restore of the latest state refuses with `Gap` until the next scheduled snapshot, up to 24 hours, behind `/healthz` 200. A documented limit of D173, not an expected-fail test.
- D2: after a failed WAL fsync the engine goes on as Go does (RUN-3, `docs/decisions/D173.md`): it keeps staging and runs the fsync again, and it keeps nothing across a restart. A power loss after the failure can leave the database behind what was staged; the next boot snapshots (`LocalRewound`). Commits the kernel dropped from the page cache are lost to SQLite too.

**Evidence.** Oracle `LatestLostAck` (the latest restore is not older than the newest transaction the engine acknowledged) and oracle `RestoreRefused` on the simulator runs listed under G1. Acknowledgements survive power loss (CHK-2, which fixed C2).

### G7. Health is not clean while replication is halted

**Statement.** If the application has committed and no commit has reached the replica for longer than the retry cap (5 minutes) plus one poll interval, then at least one of these holds: `/healthz` is not 200; `Health.degraded` is not empty; `lsr_unreplicated_age_seconds` exceeds that time.

**Counted against G7.**
- N1: the age gauge restarts from the process's start at every restart, so a crash loop shorter than the alert threshold keeps it low. Before its first boot a process counts lag from its start, so a process that starts with the store unreachable no longer shows a clean age gauge; since #104 its boot task (the sync task) is also degraded as a stall from its 8th failed boot.

D12 is closed by #104: every fault whose policy is to retry degrades its task as `Stalled(<fault>)` at the 8th failure in a row (`STALLED_AFTER` in `core/src/fault.rs`). From a 1 s interval the waits before the 8th failure sum to 127 s, inside the statement's 5 minutes. A failed WAL fsync is degraded at once (`wal_sync_failed`). A wait a maintenance level keeps never stalls; maintenance blocks no commit's way to the replica.

**Evidence.** core `every_retried_fault_stalls_and_no_stall_stops` (every `Fault` either is persistent, stalls, or is the end of a drain), core `a_retried_fault_that_persists_ends_degraded_as_a_stall` (eight injected `LocalDiskFull` on one task, then the degraded event and `Actor::degraded`). Both act on the policy and the actor; no test runs a halted engine for 5 minutes and reads `/healthz`. Mark: **D** for the routing, **untested** end to end. Tracked in issue #99.

Also in the simulator, end to end over the engine: a hold that keeps the replica from moving while the app commits, through at least twice the stall threshold of failed attempts, must end with some task degraded (oracle `HealthBlind`); sim `a_halt_long_enough_to_stall_is_reported_degraded` holds an upload outage, a full staging disk and revoked credentials for 40 ticks each; plant `stall-never-reported-sim`. Drawn holds (at most 11 ticks) rarely reach the threshold. `/healthz` and the age gauge (N1) are not read.

### Two further properties

**G5. Retention keeps a defined set of targets restorable.** Define the *retained set* of a listing at an anchor time as: the latest state; every transaction id from the last transaction of the first retained snapshot upward **that some retained file ends on**; and the restore point pinned while a task is degraded. Statement: after any delete job, or any subset of one, every target in the retained set that planned before still plans. What is *not* retained, by design: individual transactions older than the level-0 retention (5 minutes by default) once a merged file covers them. A restore to such an id refuses with `InsideFile`. So "point in time" inside the retention window means "to the granularity of the merged files that remain".
Evidence: the planner's self-check runs on every job (`retention` in `core/src/maintain/retain.rs`, "Self-check"), which is the planner checking itself; core `a_rule_that_breaks_the_newest_restore_is_caught_by_the_self_check`, `the_planner_keeps_its_promises_over_any_write_history`. Oracle `RetentionTooEager` checks ages against windows, not restorability. The simulator oracle does restore every id after deletes and accepts a refusal only "inside a kept compacted file", which is the closest thing to a judged G5. No test enumerates the retained set and restores each member. Rests on A6. Mark: **D**, by the engine's own planner.

**G6. A restore target is absent, as it was, or complete.** The database is built in `<target>.restore`, fsynced, hard-linked into place, and the directory fsynced. An existing target is replaced only when it is empty or `-force` is given, and only at publish: the restored file is renamed over it once complete and checked, after its journal files are removed and the directory fsynced; a restore that fails leaves it as it was (REST-2, D160). Evidence: cli `an_existing_output_exits_1_and_stays_byte_identical`, `a_gap_target_exits_1_names_the_hole_and_leaves_no_file`, `a_failed_forced_restore_keeps_the_old_database`; local `journal_removals_are_synced_before_the_rename`; local `a_target_created_during_the_restore_is_not_overwritten`, `a_failed_restore_leaves_no_target_and_no_scratch`. Power loss during publish is **untested**. Needs hard links.

---

## 2. Fault model

Scope has three values here. **In**: the engine is meant to keep G1 to G4 through the fault and a rig produces it. **Out**: excluded on purpose, with the reason given; an operator must prevent it. Deliberate forgery of local or replica files is out (D131): files written to pass the engine's checks. **Gap**: the fault is real, the engine is meant to cope or nobody decided, and no rig produces it. v1 filed several gaps as "out". "Rig" names what produces the fault in a test; "rig: none" is itself a finding.

The simulator can inject exactly these: `Op` (commit, spill, vacuum, shrink, other-process reader and checkpoint, crash, power loss, lying WAL sync, store fault, environment fault, clock jump, shutdown, snapshot request, staging damage, WAL damage, second writer, remote rollback, dropped created-at), `FaultShape` (error, error after publish, size or checksum mismatch, corrupt publish, flip a byte on read, fail at a rate), and `EnvFault`. A store fault fires at one call index or at a rate; **no shape holds a fault until a later step**, which is why lasting conditions have no S mark. Anything not in that list has no simulator rig.

### Local disk

| Fault | Scope | What the engine does | Rig | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Power loss, honest disk. Each block written since the last fsync of its file keeps its old or new bytes independently; the file length is drawn separately | In | Rows BOOT-3 to BOOT-11 | Block crash law at 512 and 4096 bytes: `sqlite/src/testing/vfs.rs`, sim `block_law_campaign`, sqlite `crash_cycle_campaign`. The crash-cycle rig also judges restores while the loss holds: during the segment with the loss armed, with the power off before SQLite recovers, and after the reboot before the engine settles (sqlite `crash_cycles_under_the_block_law_restore_committed_states`; the campaign of 100 seeds of 12 cycles judged 1971, 568 and 568 such restores, 0 anomalies) | The law is taken from the ALICE model (Pillai et al., OSDI 2014). **assumed**: it has not been observed on real disks |
| Power loss on the real binary | In | Same | LazyFS (`scripts/lazyfs.py`): clear-cache after kill, torn-seq and torn-op on the WAL. It also restores while the loss holds, with the power off and nothing restarted (300 cycles from seed 1000: 166 such restores, 0 anomalies) | LazyFS passes metadata operations straight to the backing filesystem. A missing directory fsync cannot be seen |
| A lost directory entry: a rename or unlink not followed by a directory fsync | In | Commit of a staged file fsyncs the file, renames, fsyncs the directory; a delete removes its files and fsyncs each level directory it named, once per call (`store-fs/src/lib.rs`) | store-fs `a_crash_at_any_step_of_a_commit_loses_nothing_committed_and_shows_nothing_torn` and `a_crash_after_a_delete_never_brings_the_file_back` replay the steps and crash at every one (plants `staging-fsync-before-rename`, `staging-dir-fsync`, `delete-without-dir-sync`). The simulator treats a delete as durable at once, which the store now makes true | A directory the store did not create, made by an earlier crashed process and never synced, is the one window left (`store-fs` module doc) |
| fsync of the WAL fails | In: nothing staged from before the retry's fsync; a power loss afterwards snapshots at the next boot | RUN-3 | sqlite `a_failed_wal_sync_stages_nothing_then_goes_on_and_a_power_loss_snapshots`, `a_restart_after_a_failed_wal_sync_boots_as_after_any_crash`; sim `wal_fsync_fails`, `wal_fsync_fails_then_restart`, `wal_fsync_fails_then_power_loss` with the simulator's lost writeback | Forgotten by a restart, as Go does (D2, accepted) |
| fsync of a staged file fails | In | RUN-15 | sim `fsync_fails_once` | |
| fsync of the database file fails inside a checkpoint | Unclear | Retried as local I/O (CKPT-8) | None | **unknown** whether SQLite's next checkpoint copies the pages again (D8) |
| Disk full | In | RUN-14 | sim `disk_full_on_staging` | Never escalates past "retrying" |
| Read error (EIO) on the WAL or database | In | RUN-13 | sim `eio_on_wal_read`; LazyFS dead-mount restores (`scripts/lazyfs.py`): a torn fault kills the filesystem under the running engine and the replica is restored meanwhile | |
| A disk that reports fsync success and keeps nothing | Out for prevention (no software can make it durable), in for detection | BOOT-9: detected only while the regrown WAL differs from what was staged | sim `power_loss_wal_rewind` (`Op::LyingWalSync`); plant `wal-barrier`; sim `a_power_loss_on_a_lying_disk_leaves_the_replica_restorable_while_it_lies` (a power loss inside a lying-disk hold, restores judged while the disk still lies) | No software can make such a disk durable. The replica is then ahead of the database (G2) |
| Bit rot in the database file | Partly | RUN-12 for a page the engine has seen, except in the snapshot after a TRUNCATE (CKPT-4). A page never seen is replicated as found | sim `db_page_flipped_under_sync` | A scrubber is not built |
| Bit rot in a staged file | In | BOOT-5, RUN-16 | sim `staged_byte_flip_before_upload`, `newest_staged_file_byte_flip` | |
| A local course's slot damaged, or the staging directory copied aside while a course runs | Not modelled | By reasoning: slot files that do not read whole or do not chain begin and continue no course (BOOT-26, BOOT-29), and a boot moves them up and judges them as any pending file (BOOT-27) | None: the simulator's staging damage and its saved copy touch level 0 only | **untested** |
| Torn write inside a block (old 512e disks) | Out. Reason: 4Kn and 512e-with-atomic-sector drives are the norm; SQLite's own WAL checksums catch a torn frame | n/a | n/a | The block law has no sub-block tear, so this is asserted, not shown |
| Filesystems where locks or shared mmap do not work (NFS, virtiofs, some container bind mounts) | **Gap.** SQLite itself is unsafe there, but users do it and the engine does not refuse | Not detected | None | **unknown**. A startup self-test or a documented refusal is missing (A2) |

### SQLite and other processes

| Fault | Scope | What the engine does | Rig |
|---|---|---|---|
| The application's own automatic or manual checkpoints | In | Counted and reported as a notice; they cannot pass the engine's read mark (RUN-22) | sim `app_autocheckpoints`; sim `rules_match_sqlite` compares the model with real SQLite |
| The application commits (growing the database or not), begins a write transaction it leaves open, begins or ends a reader, checkpoints in any mode or deletes the WAL between two of the engine's calls into SQLite inside one tick | In | The read-lock proofs (RUN-7, CKPT-1): what lands before a committed read is in the step, what lands after waits for the next. A write transaction left open holds the write lock at the engine's barrier or its sequence bump (CKPT-10, CKPT-11) | sim `Op::AppInTick` in swarm mixes (`sim/src/sqlite/in_tick.rs`), at any call of the tick, reads included; `Op::CommitInTick` for the one commit before the checkpoint's barrier |
| Another process checkpoints while the engine is down | In | BOOT-7, BOOT-8 | sim `app_checkpoint_while_down`; sqlite `the_apps_last_close_while_the_replicator_is_down_deletes_the_wal` |
| WAL deleted, truncated or its header torn | In | BOOT-7, RUN-5, RUN-6, RUN-9 | sim `wal_deleted_while_down`, `wal_header_torn`, `wal_truncated_externally_under_lock`, `wal_unlinked_mid_run` |
| WAL rewritten with other bytes in the same generation | In | BOOT-9, BOOT-10 | sim `wal_regrown_same_salts_diff_bytes`, `wal_rewritten_same_length`; core `a_rewound_tail_rewritten_with_the_same_last_page_is_not_continued` |
| A committed WAL frame flips a bit | In | RUN-4 | sim `bit_flip_in_committed_frame`, `bit_flip_in_committed_frame_healed` |
| Page size changes (`VACUUM` with a new `page_size`) | In | RUN-11, BOOT-15 | sim `vacuum_page_size_change`, `vacuum_page_size_change_and_back` |
| The process links a SQLite outside the verified builds: a system library or a `libsqlite3-sys` outside 0.37 to 0.40, SQLCipher, a loadable-extension build | Partly | The SQLite this crate's calls reach is checked at open, by version number and source id against the verified builds (3.51.3, 3.53.1, 3.53.2, 3.54.0, 3.54.1) its private-layout reads have been checked against; any other refuses to start (`StartError::SqliteBuild`, naming the build found and the builds verified). Each listed build runs the whole lsr-sqlite suite in CI (`docs/decisions/D200.md`). A second copy the application links for its own connections is not detected: it breaks the one-SQLite rule (`crates/sqlite/src/lib.rs`, module doc) | sqlite `another_version_or_build_of_sqlite_is_refused`, `the_linked_build_is_accepted_exactly_when_it_is_listed`, `every_listed_build_is_accepted` |
| Journal mode changed away from WAL while the engine runs | In by SQLite's own rule | The engine's read transaction blocks it | sim `our_reader_blocks_leaving_wal_mode` |
| Journal mode changed away from WAL while the engine is down | In | Start puts the database back in WAL mode, or refuses with `NotWal` (CFG-4); the first sync is a snapshot | **untested** end to end |
| The database file replaced by another file at the same path | Partly | While running: the WAL's device and inode no longer match, `wal_unlinked` (RUN-6). While down: a snapshot, since the WAL cannot continue | replica `a_database_replaced_after_a_panic_is_degraded_and_not_reopened`. Inode number reuse making a different file look the same: **unknown** |
| A process that closes a file descriptor on the database inside the application's process | Out. Reason: POSIX semantics give a library no way to see or stop it; it is an embedding rule | POSIX locks for the whole process drop. Not detected | None. The embedding rules forbid it (`crates/sqlite/src/lib.rs`, module doc) |
| `synchronous=NORMAL` (the usual WAL setting) and power loss | In | SQLite leaves committed frames unsynced until its next checkpoint, so power loss takes back acknowledged commits: SQLite's contract. The engine fsyncs the WAL before staging, so the replica does not run ahead on an honest disk; after the loss the boot continues or snapshots by the boot rows. The LazyFS rig sees the database lose acknowledged commits in most runs | LazyFS; sqlite `power_loss_at_each_wal_sync_loses_no_acknowledged_transaction` |
| `synchronous=OFF` | Partly. SQLite itself promises no crash consistency at OFF, so there is no committed history to be faithful to | An application connection checked with `check_app_connection` is refused (`SetupError::SynchronousOff`). A connection the check never sees, or another process's, is not detected (A10). The replicator's own connections set FULL, so its checkpoints sync whatever the build's default | sqlite `check_refuses_a_connection_that_never_syncs`, `the_replicator_s_connections_sync_at_full` |

### The process

| Fault | Scope | What the engine does | Rig |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kill at any await point (`kill -9`, OOM) | In | Next boot recovers (section 3, boot rows) | sim `seeds_1_to_8` (crash operations in the fixed seeds); sqlite `a_writer_killed_mid_transaction_leaves_no_page_in_any_file`, `a_writer_killed_before_the_wal_index_update_is_never_replicated`; compat alternation with a kill at a random point |
| Panic inside a tick | In | RUN-20 | replica `a_panic_keeps_what_is_left_in_health_and_the_deadline_report` |
| Crash loop: the process dies every few seconds | In for correctness, **known wrong** for cost | BOOT-20 | LazyFS measurement only; no assertion |
| A restart right after a fault was reported | Not modelled | Retry counts, waits, degraded states and stops are all forgotten (BOOT-21) | None. **untested** |
| `fork()` of the host process | In | The child reports `Gone::Forked` and replicates nothing | **code only** (`replica/src/health.rs`) |

| Fault | Scope | What the engine does | Rig |

| Failover with the old host still alive | In: the second put of an id is refused and that host stops | WORLD-5, UP-14, UP-17 | core `failover_with_the_old_host_alive_never_publishes_one_id_twice` |

| The host application exits before a drain returns, or starves the replicator thread | **Gap** | WORLD-30 | None |

### The clock

| Fault | Scope | What the engine does | Rig |
|---|---|---|---|
| Steps back across a restart | **Gap** | The clamp is per process. Created-at times can run backwards across files (WORLD-13) | None. **untested** |
| Steps back | In | Engine time keeps its value, then runs at half the wall clock's rate until the wall clock catches up, after twice the step; each step back is reported once (RUN-19). Timers slow down for that window and never stop. Stamps stay on the wall clock, never below its highest reading | sim `clock_steps_back_10s`, `clock_steps_back_an_hour_mid_retry`; core `a_backward_clock_step_does_not_freeze_a_retry_wait`, `clock_back_clamped_once` |
| Steps forward | In for retention, out for as-of | Believed. Retention ages are measured from `min(now, newest created-at in the listing)`, so one bad reading deletes nothing the replica's own newest time would not (`core/src/maintain/retain.rs`). But files staged during the jump carry the future time, which raises that anchor | sim `clock_jumps_forward_an_hour` (no fault, every acknowledged commit restores); oracle `RetentionTooEager` judges against the simulator's own clock, so it shares the premise. Early deletion by the size of a forward jump is **untested** |
| The store's clock differs from the host's, as it affects retention | In, by construction | Ages never come from a listed last-modified time (`retain.rs`) | core `last_modified_matched_only_in_allowed_sites` |

| A second writer on the same prefix | In, detected late while running; at boot when it wrote while this engine was down | UP-10, UP-14, BOOT-22 | sim `second_writer_mid_run`, `maintenance_stops_on_another_writer`, `foreign_file_while_down_stops` |
| Listings that lag the bucket | Not modelled: a store whose listings lag is unsupported (D173) | Not handled (BOOT-18, MAINT-12) | `consistency_probe` on MinIO: 0 stale results in 1000 rounds. On Tigris, AWS S3 and R2 (2026-11-24, from one region): 0 stale listings or reads in 1000 writes each, and 0 after a 2500-key delete (`provider::a_second_connection_lists_and_reads_each_of_1000_writes_at_once`); Tigris cross-region and long runs not measured |

---

## 3. The behaviour table

Responses are one of: **continue** (no cost), **snapshot** (the next file is every page of the database), **retry** (the failing task waits and tries again; the wait doubles from 1 s to a cap of 5 minutes, `core/src/fault.rs`), **degraded** (a retry that is also reported as a persistent failure in `Health.degraded`, `/healthz` and `lsr_degraded`), **stop** (the engine does no further I/O until restarted; `/healthz` answers 503), **refuse** (a command or a start fails).

Five tasks keep separate retry waits and degraded states: sync, checkpoint, upload, prune, maintenance (`Task` in `core/src/actor/state.rs`). They are not independent. A boot fault holds all five. A checkpoint never runs without a sync that reached the committed end in the same tick. Maintenance runs only while uploads are idle, so an upload backlog or an upload fault holds merges, retention and the read-back (MAINT-13). A level-9 snapshot waits on the sync task.

### Health signals

| Signal | Where | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `Health.degraded[task]`, `/healthz` `degraded[]`, `lsr_degraded{task,cause}` | `replica/src/health.rs`, `sidecar/src/health.rs` | A persistent cause the task keeps retrying. Cause names: `credentials`, `permission_denied`, `clock_skew`, `bucket_missing`, `tls`, `store_refused`, `remote_corrupt`, `other_writer`, `remote_regressed`, `wal_corrupt`, `wal_truncated`, `wal_unlinked`, `wal_sync_failed`, `page_size_changed`, `database_page_mismatch`, `bug`; and for a stall (a retried fault that failed 8 times in a row), the fault's name: `store_transient`, `store_throttled`, `remote_object_missing`, `upload_ambiguous`, `upload_checksum_mismatch`, `staged_lost`, `local_disk_full`, `local_io`, `local_fsync_failed`, `sqlite_busy`, `readers_held`, `wal_moved`, `wal_index_inconsistent`, `wal_changed_under_read`, `nothing_to_snapshot`, `read_tx_lost` (`cause_name`, `stall_name` in `sidecar/src/health.rs`) |
| `Health.phase`, `lsr_db_state`, `lsr_stopped`, `/healthz` status | `sidecar/src/health.rs` | 200 while booting, running, draining, degraded or waiting for a file. 503 once any database stopped, failed to start, or lost its thread |
| `lsr_retries_total`, log line "retrying" | `sidecar/src/logging.rs` | A fault whose policy is to retry, for its first 7 failures in a row on one task. From the 8th the task is degraded as a stall (row above) and stays so until it succeeds. No retried fault stops the engine |
| `lsr_resnapshot_pending{cause}`, log "snapshot required", boot log `cause=` | `sidecar/src/health.rs` | The next file is a snapshot, and why |
| `lsr_unreplicated_age_seconds`, `lsr_replicated_txid`, `lsr_wal_bytes` | `Health` in `replica/src/health.rs` | Lag and local growth. The age gauge is 0 only when a booted engine shows the replica caught up; before that it counts from the process's start (N1) |
| `lsr_verify_failing`, `lsr_verify_failing_txid`, `lsr_verify_failing_page` | `replica/src/health.rs` | The scheduled verify found the replica restores something the live database never held |
| `lsr_app_notice`, `lsr_app_checkpoints_total` | `replica/src/health.rs` | The application ran checkpoints of its own. A notice, not a failure |
| `lsr_panics_total` | `replica/src/health.rs` | Panics caught at the thread boundary |
| `lsr_read_lock_violations_total` | `replica/src/health.rs` | Recoveries that found commits lost to a WAL restart the read lock should have prevented: a broken lock or a bug |
| `lsr_scratch_sweeps_denied_total` | `replica/src/health.rs` | 1 once the replica refused the start-up sweep of leftover uploads for want of permission (skipped; replication is unaffected) |

There is no per-cause count of snapshots taken on main. It is on #86 (`lsr_snapshots_total{db,cause}`).

### Fault index

<!-- enum: Fault -->

Every `Fault` variant (`core/src/fault.rs`), its policy (`policy`, `fault.rs`, one exhaustive match), and the rows that describe it. sim `every_fault_has_a_scenario_or_a_reason` asserts each variant has a scenario or a stated reason for none. One variant is covered by a reason only: `Bug`, on the ground that oracle `BugFault` fails every run that meets it (`fault_reason` in `sim/src/scenario/faults.rs`). `WalSyncFailed` has scenarios under every response set. Having a scenario means D at most; see the marks.

Every policy written "retry" below also stalls: from the 8th failure in a row on one task the task is degraded `Stalled(<fault>)`, keeps the fault's own wait, and never stops (`Actor::stalled` in `core/src/actor.rs`). core `every_retried_fault_stalls_and_no_stall_stops` checks every variant.

| `Fault` | Policy | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| `StoreTransient` | retry | UP-2, UP-6 |
| `StoreThrottled` | retry, waiting at least what the store named | UP-3 |
| `StoreDenied(Denied)` | persistent: `StoreDenied` of the same kind | UP-4, WORLD-15, WORLD-23 |
| `RemoteObjectMissing` | retry | UP-12, MAINT-2 |
| `UploadAmbiguous` | retry | UP-7 |
| `UploadChecksumMismatch` | retry | UP-8 |
| `StoreRefused` | persistent: `StoreRefused` | UP-5, UP-13, UP-21 |
| `RemoteCorrupt` | persistent: `RemoteCorrupt` | BOOT-14, BOOT-17, MAINT-3, MAINT-4 |
| `RemoteAheadOfLocal` | persistent: `OtherWriter` | UP-10, MAINT-10 |
| `RemoteRegressed` | persistent: `RemoteRegressed` | UP-11 |
| `StagedLost` | retry, then a snapshot on the second failed read | BOOT-5, RUN-16, RUN-17, RUN-18 |
| `LocalDiskFull` | retry | RUN-14, UP-15 |
| `LocalIo` | retry | RUN-13, BOOT-12, CKPT-8 |
| `LocalFsyncFailed` | retry | RUN-15 |
| `WalSyncFailed` | persistent: `WalSyncFailed` | RUN-3 |
| `SqliteBusy` | retry | RUN-13, CKPT-5 |
| `ReadersHeld` | retry | CKPT-3 |
| `WalMoved` | retry | RUN-13, RUN-8 |
| `WalIndexInconsistent` | retry | RUN-13 |
| `WalChangedUnderRead` | retry | RUN-13 |
| `NothingToSnapshot` | retry | RUN-13 |
| `ReadTxLost` | retry | CKPT-7 |
| `PageSizeChanged` | persistent: `PageSizeChanged` | BOOT-15, RUN-11 |
| `WalCorruptBelowCommitted` | persistent: `WalCorrupt` | RUN-4 |
| `WalTruncatedBelowCommitted` | persistent: `WalTruncated` | RUN-5 |
| `WalUnlinked` | persistent: `WalUnlinked` | RUN-6 |
| `DatabasePageMismatch` | persistent: `DatabasePageMismatch` | RUN-12 |
| `ShutdownDeadline` | stop: `Shutdown`, not configurable | RUN-24 |
| `Bug` | persistent: `Bug` | RUN-20 |

### Persistent causes and their configured responses

<!-- enum: PersistentCause -->

`Responses` (`core/src/fault.rs`) has one field per cause but `Stalled`, which is not configurable. `GO_LIKE` is the default: keep trying on every cause, stop on another writer. `STRICT` stops on every cause. Evidence that each preset routes every cause as it says: sim `under_strict_every_persistent_scenario_stops_with_its_cause`, `under_go_like_every_persistent_scenario_degrades_and_the_oracle_holds`. Most of those scenarios inject one error; they show routing (D), not behaviour under a lasting cause.

| `PersistentCause` | Default | What "keep trying" does (`action`, `fault.rs`) | Clears when |
|---|---|---|---|
| `StoreDenied(Denied)` | keep trying | Retries the same step. One response per kind: `credentials`, `permission_denied`, `clock_skew`, `bucket_missing`, `tls` | The step succeeds |
| `StoreRefused` | keep trying | Retries the same step | The step succeeds |
| `RemoteCorrupt` | keep trying | Retries the same step | The object verifies, or is repaired (MAINT-4) |
| `OtherWriter` | **stop** | With `ContinueAndInterleaveHistories`: drops all engine state, boots again, and snapshots after the other writer's newest transaction | n/a under the default |
| `RemoteRegressed` | keep trying | Lists the replica again, continues from what it holds now, and sends again the staged files it lacks | The relist succeeds |
| `WalCorrupt` | keep trying | Rescans. Nothing is staged past the bad frame | The frame verifies, or the WAL starts a new generation |
| `WalTruncated` | keep trying | Rescans | The WAL covers its committed end again, or a new generation |
| `WalUnlinked` | keep trying | Retries | Never by itself: the file is gone. An operator restarts the application and the replicator |
| `WalSyncFailed` | keep trying (`litestream-rs.responses.wal-sync-failed`) | The step that met it stages nothing. The next sync step runs the fsync again and remembers nothing, as Go would (RUN-3, `docs/decisions/D173.md`) | An fsync succeeds |
| `PageSizeChanged` | keep trying | Retries. Never writes the new page size | The page size returns, or a restart onto a new prefix |
| `DatabasePageMismatch` | keep trying | Ships the page as the file holds it; stays degraded | A verified WAL frame rewrites the page, the page table is emptied by a snapshot, or an operator calls `accept_database_pages` |
| `Bug` | keep trying | Drops all engine state and boots again after the wait | The fresh boot succeeds |
| `Stalled(<fault>)` | keep trying, not configurable, never a stop | Retries on the fault's own wait. Raised at the 8th failure in a row of a retried fault on one task | The task's next success |

While any task is degraded, retention pins the newest transaction the replica held when the first degradation began, and keeps its whole restore chain (`core/src/actor.rs`, `maintain/retain.rs`).

### Boot

A boot runs on the first tick of every process, and again after a rebuild. In order: list the replica and read back its newest level-0 file; sweep the staged files; read and check the pending staged files (each whole, chained to the one before); read the newest staged file; decide (`core/src/actor.rs`). A failure at any step starts the boot again from the top after the sync task's wait. Nothing is uploaded before the boot finishes.

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BOOT-1 | Staging holds no level-0 file and the replica is empty (`recover.rs`) | snapshot, `FirstSync` | `first_sync` | None | Automatic | core::`rule_1_no_newest_staged_file_is_a_first_sync`, sim::`empty_meta_boot` |  | sim::`empty_meta_boot` |  |  |  |
| BOOT-2 | Staging is empty and the replica holds files. The replicator emits `Event::Refused { through }`, and the sidecar logs "replicate refused: the replica holds transactions up to {txid} that this database has no record of writing. Another replicator may be using this prefix. If this database is the right one, run `lsr trust-local <db> --replica <url>` and start again. If the replica is the right one, restore from it with `lsr restore`." Every later start stops again until one of: this database is the right one, and `lsr trust-local <db> --replica <url>` records a takeover through the replica's newest transaction (snapshot above it, BOOT-6); the replica is the right one, and `lsr restore` gets its state (it writes the takeover record itself); the files are removed. Ids alone cannot tell another writer's file from this engine's own lost one, so a doubt keeps the stop. The engine's own deletes of staged files keep a takeover record first (BOOT-30), so a refusal with no other writer comes only from an operator's action (a meta directory deleted after a WAL restart whose database is no longer the replica's state, a VM put back): the swarm's judge fails any other. A brand-new meta directory over a database equal to the replica's state is not refused (BOOT-6, D170), nor one written after the replica's newest file that holds at least half of its pages, the commits a lost meta directory held and never uploaded (BOOT-6, D194); an empty or unrelated database, or one last written before the replica's newest file, still is. Any other failure of the sweep (a timeout, a refusal of the credentials, the clock or the bucket) fails the boot attempt as before | log `the replica refused the sweep of leftover uploads at start` once; `Event::ScratchSweepDenied`; `lsr_scratch_sweeps_denied_total` 1; `Counters::scratch_sweeps_denied` | Storage only: an upload a killed process left open stays open and billed until a bucket lifecycle rule or an operator removes it | Grant the two actions or add an `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload` lifecycle rule (docs/s3-permissions.md) | core::`a_refused_scratch_sweep_does_not_hold_the_boot`, core::`a_refused_scratch_sweep_is_not_asked_again`, core::`only_a_permission_refusal_of_the_scratch_sweep_is_skipped`, sim::`a_refused_scratch_sweep_never_holds_the_boot`, sim::`a_permission_refusal_of_the_boot_sweep_is_skipped_and_reports_no_refusal`, sim::`only_a_permission_refusal_of_the_sweep_is_exempt`, compat::`s3::over_s3_lsr_runs_on_the_policy_litestreams_guide_publishes`, plant::`scratch-sweep-refusal-stops-boot`, plant::`boot-swallows-a-denied-listing`, plant::`boot-sweep-skip-not-noted` |  | sim::`a_refused_scratch_sweep_never_holds_the_boot` |  | Go sweeps nothing, so a drop-in replacement must start under Go's policy. Found by a review of what a first start under that policy does; a refusal here said nothing about the replica's contents, yet it held the application's writes under `hold-start`. The simulator's refusal judge exempts exactly this: a Permission refusal of ListMultipartUploads or an abort while the engine has not judged its boot, counted as `http_fault.sweep_denied`, and then demands the engine noted the skip; every other refusal, a refused listing of the replica included, is still owed a report |  |

Staging holds only what a read made after an fsync that succeeded gave, but after a failed writeback the kernel calls the pages clean and the next fsync's success covers nothing, so a restore can then mix two histories (issue #364, N2). | RUN-34 | A level-9 snapshot read from the database is larger than the bytes one tick sends (8 MiB, `UploadConfig::bytes_per_tick`) | continue. The snapshot goes to the replica a slice a tick, each slice at most the tick's bytes; after every eighth slice the rest of the engine has a tick, so commits made meanwhile are synced and uploaded before the snapshot is whole, as under Go. A slice whose upload waits for its parts hands nothing over and the tick goes on to other work; on S3 the upload says it waits before the write that would fill a part with every lane taken, so no slice's write waits for a part on the engine's thread. No checkpoint of the engine's runs until the snapshot ends (Go's checkpoint lock does the same), so the read transaction it began under is held throughout. Each slice first reads the wal-index again; another hold of the read transaction, another WAL generation, a backfill past the snapshot's synced end or an emptied page checksum table ends it, its upload aborted, and the job is retried on its schedule. A database-file page is checked against the table as it stood when the snapshot began. A drain, a stop, a rebuild and a crash end it; a drain runs no maintenance (`docs/decisions/D255.md`) | `Event::Sending { bytes }` for each slice; a fault ends it as the snapshot job's failure | None. The staging of a whole database at level 0 is still one unit, as Go's sync is; the snapshot a `replicate -once -force-snapshot` drain ends with is written whole, in one call. The checkpoints the snapshot holds off grow the WAL for its length, as under Go. The simulator stamps a snapshot's upload when it begins (`sim/src/oracle/as_of.rs`) and does not count its slices against the ticks a boot may take to settle |  |

### Checkpoint

The engine runs every checkpoint itself, in the same tick as a sync that reached the committed end, and never without that sync's proof (`core/src/actor.rs`).

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CKPT-1 | 1000 synced frames, or 1 minute since the last checkpoint with something synced (`checkpoint.rs`). PASSIVE under a write barrier: seal (sync through the committed end), checkpoint, write one frame of its own, follow the WAL if it restarted (`checkpoint.rs`) | continue. A store fully down for one operation (list, read, upload or delete) for 2 to 11 ticks while the app commits: both judges restore the raw replica in the middle of the outage and at its end, and restores made through the down store fail with a store or listing error, never with another database (oracle `HeldRestore`). Campaign in `sim/tests/sim/survival.rs`: 2000 seeds of the default mix with holds, 0 failures. Network: the `network::` tests run the real replicator through Toxiproxy to MinIO (`just test-s3`) with a reset part way through a request or response body, a response that never arrives after the store acted, bodies sliced into small writes, and a slow link with latency; the history judge judges restores read straight from MinIO while the fault holds and after it clears. No planted bug is killed by them yet: the nightly ledger run starts no services |  |

| UP-10 | A listing made while running shows the replica ahead of everything this engine staged (`upload.rs`, `actor.rs`) | **stop**, `OtherWriter` (default). No upload, compaction or delete follows. Files one of this engine's own running drops vouched for (BOOT-30: the replica may hold them unconfirmed) are not another writer's: until the next boot a listing judges the replica against them as well as what is staged, the uploader lists first after the drop so they are confirmed and the snapshot goes above them, and a divergence at such an id is the engine's own earlier upload, which stands. Nor are files at or below what this process confirmed: a listing after a rollback (UP-11) judges the replica against that mark too, so the file a boot took as its base is not taken for another writer's once the rollback took the files above it. Cost accepted (D51): after a rollback, a second writer that refills such an id with no staged copy to compare is taken as the replica's file | `other_writer`; `/healthz` 503 | Everything committed after the stop is unreplicated until an operator acts. The steady state makes no listing, so the other writer is seen only at the next maintenance listing or after an ambiguous commit. A maintenance listing follows this engine's own upload by at most one compaction window (30 s by default) once uploads are idle; an engine with nothing to upload lists only when its next scheduled job is due. Every maintenance listing includes level 0 whole, so another writer's level-0 file is seen by the pass that lists it; level 0 is listed at every retention check (the set `l0-retention-check-interval`, 120 s when unset; MAINT-14, D193). A file at level 1 to 8 below the newest this engine knows there, a foreign snapshot, a file at a level above the configured top (never listed by a short pass) and a replica that lost files below the tail wait for the next whole listing, at most 15 minutes. A task holds one degraded cause, so while another fault holds it health names that one. store-fs's hard-link create-only on a filesystem without links is an open question (D79) |  |
| UP-20 | A staged file is longer than 10000 parts of the configured part size (48.8 GiB at the default 5 MiB part, 977 GiB at 100 MB). An upload with no file behind it (a compaction, a level-9 snapshot) holds up to `concurrency` parts, so its bound is `concurrency x max(part size, length / 10000)`; at the 5 TiB ceiling that is about 524 MB a part, 5.5 GB at a concurrency of 10 | Automatic | store-s3::`an_object_over_ten_thousand_configured_parts_fills_a_larger_part`, store-s3::`an_object_that_fits_is_cut_at_the_configured_part`, store-s3::`every_object_up_to_the_limit_fits_in_ten_thousand_legal_parts`, store-s3::`the_part_grows_only_when_the_configured_one_does_not_fit`, core::`an_upload_is_begun_with_the_staged_files_length`; plant::`part-size-ignores-length` |  |  |  | Before, such a file uploaded 10000 parts, was refused at part 10001 (`StoreRefused`), waited and sent the same bytes again without end, each try costing 48.8 GiB of egress at the default part. | UP-21 | A staged file is longer than 5 TiB, S3's limit for one object, so no part size carries it | The upload is refused before a byte is sent, `StoreError::ObjectTooLarge` (the key, the length, the limit), and reported as `StoreRefused`. Staged files accumulate on the staging disk | Operator | store-s3::`an_object_over_the_store_limit_is_refused_before_a_byte_is_sent`, store-s3::`an_object_of_exactly_the_store_limit_begins`, store-s3::`the_largest_object_is_carried_and_one_byte_more_is_refused`, core::`an_object_over_the_store_limit_is_a_store_refusal`, core::`an_object_over_the_store_limit_is_permanent`; plant::`object-over-the-limit-begins` |  |  |  | Before, the refusal came from S3 at part 10001, after every part was sent. | UP-23 | The S3 store keeps no part of a staged file it is handed: of each part it keeps the length and CRC-64 of every 256 KiB the engine read, and reads the part from the staging directory again on the thread that sends it, once to take its SHA-256 and once as the request's body. Between the engine's read and a later one the staged file changed, or a later read failed | A later read is given to the store only when each 256 KiB of it has the length and CRC-64 the engine's read saw, on both later reads, before a byte of it is hashed or sent; every request is signed with the SHA-256 of what it sends (`docs/decisions/D233.md`, `docs/decisions/D250.md`). A second read that differs ends the upload before anything is published, as `StagedLost` (RUN-16 then applies: the file is read again from its first byte, and dropped for a snapshot when it fails a second time). One that fails ends it the same way as the staging directory's fault (`LocalIo`). Neither is reported as the replica's, and neither costs a listing or a delete: nothing was published | "retrying", then none or `newest_staged_file_unreadable` | None: no byte of a second read reaches the store unverified. An upload of a staged file reads it twice | Automatic | core::`a_staged_file_changed_behind_the_send_is_lost_and_nothing_is_published`, core::`a_second_read_that_differs_inside_a_write_ends_the_send_as_a_lost_staged_file`, core::`a_second_read_that_fails_is_the_staging_directorys_fault`, core::`a_commit_the_store_failed_for_its_own_reason_is_the_replicas_fault`, core::`a_second_read_is_one_bounded_read_that_must_be_what_was_seen`, store-s3::`every_streamed_request_carries_the_digest_and_length_of_the_first_read`, store-s3::`a_second_read_that_differs_fails_the_write_that_sends_the_part`, store-s3::`a_later_read_that_gives_nothing_fails_the_commit_before_anything_is_published`, store-s3::`a_source_that_breaks_its_word_publishes_nothing`, store-s3::`an_upload_from_a_source_sends_what_a_buffered_upload_sends`, store-s3::`an_upload_from_a_file_at_a_large_part_size_holds_no_part`, sim::`sliced::a_staged_file_changed_between_its_two_reads_is_refused_at_the_second`; oracle::`SecondReadDiffers`, oracle::`PublishedPastRefusedRead`, oracle::`RefusedReadBlamedOnReplica`; plant::`second-read-trusted-on-a-mismatch`, plant::`second-read-trusted-on-a-mismatch-sim`, plant::`streamed-part-without-its-digest`, plant::`part-reread-from-the-wrong-offset`, plant::`store-trusts-a-changed-cell` |  |  |  | Go hands its SDK the staged file and each part is read from it when sent, unverified (`s3/replica_client.go`, `uploadSizedLTX`). Here each later read is checked against the engine's read, by the engine's reader and again by the store, and the server checks the body against its signed SHA-256. Only the ureq client streams the body; the reqwest client still buffers each part. The in-memory S3 server of the tests checks the signed hash as a real server does, and `a_body_other_than_the_signed_one_is_refused_by_minio` shows a real one refusing, with `an_upload_from_a_source_round_trips` for the whole path. Compactions and level-9 snapshots have no file behind them and still hold their parts (`docs/decisions/D222.md`) |  |

### Maintenance and retention

Maintenance runs only when every staged file is uploaded (`actor.rs`). Each job is planned from a listing made in its own tick.

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |

| MAINT-1 | A level's window opened (30 s, 5 min, 1 h by default): merge at most 32 contiguous files from the level below, starting at the transaction after the level's newest file (`compact.rs`). Inputs are never deleted by the merge. A hole in the source ends the run (MAINT-15). A level with no file starts above the newest snapshot, so a first start does not carry the database through levels 1 to 3 (D224) | continue | Event `Compacted` | None. A crash leaves the inputs, and the output either visible or absent | n/a | core::`inputs_are_capped_at_the_fan_in`, core::`level_one_starts_above_the_snapshot_and_stays_contiguous`, core::`a_level_with_files_takes_what_the_snapshot_holds_too`, core::`the_first_start_uploads_the_database_twice_and_downloads_none`, compat::`levels_start_above_the_first_snapshot`, compat::`go_compacts_over_levels_that_start_above_the_snapshot`, core::`a_hole_in_the_source_is_the_same_hole_in_the_destination`, core::`restoring_the_snapshot_and_the_chain_equals_restoring_the_compacted_file`, sim::`a_level_built_after_the_engine_replaced_the_file_holds_its_pages`; plant::`level-takes-the-snapshots-range` |  | sim::`a_level_built_after_the_engine_replaced_the_file_holds_its_pages` |  |  |  |

| MAINT-14 | A maintenance pass runs on a replica with a long history (20,000 level-1 files at a week of retention) | Between whole listings a pass lists only what its decision reads: level 0, a compaction level's tail and the level below it while its window is open, level 1 after a cover cursor when level-0 retention is due and level 9 when the snapshot is due; a pass with nothing due lists nothing, and the pass after this engine's own compaction reuses its listing. The engine remembers the newest file at each level 1 to 8 (in memory, learned from whole listings and its own confirmed compactions); it deletes only level-0 files, and only ones the level 1 to 8 files listed in the pass cover whole (Go deletes by level 1's newest transaction alone), aged against an anchor that reads every remembered newest file; it reads no snapshot. Level-0 retention is checked every `l0-retention-check-interval` as set (120 s when unset), from a listing made in the same pass, never a carried one; with a remembered file above the configured top it is decided on whole listings only. A whole listing runs at boot, after any fault, refused upload, step back of the clock or a compaction that found an input gone, before a snapshot or a delete above level 0, when the retention gate was held, when snapshot retention is due, and every 15 minutes. The records are dropped on any fault, stop or restart; nothing is written to disk (D193, D254) | none | A change by another writer at level 1 to 8 below the newest known file, a foreign snapshot, any file at a level above the configured top, or a file removed below the tail, is seen at the next whole listing, at most 15 minutes later (UP-10); levels above the top are never listed by a short pass (D193). A level-0 delete made on the last whole listing's clear gate can run up to 15 minutes on that gate | Automatic: the next whole listing | 10,773 requests and 2,952,201 entries an hour at 2,900 files, about 25,000 requests at 20,000; after: 610 and 161,238, and 679 and 229,638 (about 0.45 and 0.5 million requests a month, from 7.9 and 18.3 million) |  |
| MAINT-15 | A level 1 to 8 has a hole above the next level's newest file: a file was removed from outside, or a version before D228 compacted only the newest file below (two level-1 files in one window) | A pass that lists only tails plans nothing for the level above and lists the whole replica. A whole listing closes the first such hole before any other job, whatever the windows: it compacts the files the level below holds inside the hole into the hole's level (that level's own hole first, down to level 0), then lists again, until none is left. A fill that fails waits out the backoff of the level it writes, and the level above the hole waits with it, with no whole listing meanwhile (MAINT-11); no hole is closed or passed while a level at or below the hole's holds a rejected output, whose delete goes first. A range no lower level holds in files that line up is passed as before: the next job starts after it, and the level above has the same hole (MAINT-11). A short pass's compaction that would not start at the transaction after its level's newest file is `Bug` (RUN-20), checked before anything is written | Event `Compacted` for each file written again | None on the replica: restores and retention read all levels together, so a hole a lower level covers costs neither. Go's compaction into the level above refuses (`non-contiguous transaction ids in input files`) at every tick while the hole is there, so a replica handed back to Go stops compacting at that level until the files below the hole are pruned | Automatic at the next whole listing: at start, and at most 15 minutes of maintenance passes later. ### Restore

`restore` is a free function and the `lsr restore` command. It lists the replica once, plans a chain, applies it oldest first, and publishes only after every file verified. Exit codes: 0 success, 2 refused, 3 environment, 64 usage (`cli/src/run.rs`).

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REST-1 | A chain from a file that begins at transaction 1 reaches the target exactly (`plan.rs`) | The database is built in `<target>.restore`, fsynced, hard-linked to the target; the directory is fsynced | exit 0 | None | n/a | core::`restore_equals_the_model_at_every_transaction`, core::`applies_files_oldest_first_and_newest_page_wins`, cli::`every_target_matches_the_manifest` |  |  |  |  |  |
| REST-2 | Something is at the output: a file, or a `-wal`, `-shm` or `-journal` beside it | An empty regular file is replaced at publish, as Go replaces it. Any other file, or a journal file, is refused unless `-force` is given; with `-force` the old file and its journal files stay in place until the restored database is complete and checked, then the journal files are removed (directory fsynced) and the restored file renamed over the old one (directory fsynced). A directory is never replaced. Go removes the old file and journal files before it restores (D160) | exit 1, as Go; `AlreadyExists` naming the path | None | Pass `-force`, remove the file, or run `--clear-journal-files` | cli::`an_existing_output_exits_1_and_stays_byte_identical`, cli::`a_stale_journal_file_refuses_until_clear_journal_files`, cli::`force_replaces_an_existing_database_and_its_journal_files`, cli::`a_failed_forced_restore_keeps_the_old_database`, replica::`journal_removals_are_synced_before_the_rename`, replica::`each_failed_replace_step_says_whether_the_target_is_visible`, replica::`a_target_filled_during_the_restore_is_not_replaced_in_empty_mode`, compat::`go_and_lsr_restore_the_same_command_lines`; plant::`force-removes-before-restoring`, plant::`replace-renames-before-journals-synced` |  |  |  |  |  |
| REST-3 | No exact plan (`PlanOutcome`, `plan.rs`): `EmptyReplica`, `NoSnapshot`, `BeyondNewest`, `Gap`, `InsideFile`, `MalformedSnapshot`, `AsOfInsideFile` | refuse, naming the reason and the transaction or file. `AsOfInsideFile` alone can be accepted with `--allow-earlier` | exit 1, as Go | None. Before, the open truncated the scratch, which was the target, and pages of the new file were written into it before the file's checksum was checked | none | None | Run again | replica::`a_failed_forced_restore_leaves_a_target_that_shares_the_scratch_inode_as_it_was`, replica::`a_forced_restore_over_a_target_that_shares_the_scratch_inode_replaces_it_whole`, plant::`restore-scratch-written-through-the-target` |  |  |  | The kill's on-disk state is made by hand with a hard link in the tests. | REST-18 | The replica's endpoint takes connections and never answers, or answers every listing with a failure, during a `restore` (or its `-dry-run`) | Every listing of the restore together is held to 120 seconds from the first (`RESTORE_LISTING_BUDGET`): a failed listing is retried under the usual policy until the next retry would begin at or after that time, then the restore fails. A request already in flight is not cut short, so the bound is 120 seconds plus one request deadline (60 seconds by default). Before, each level was retried five times at the request deadline in turn, about 33 minutes with a silent endpoint. The retry rules of replication and of the restore's reads are unchanged. Go retries nothing here (D211) | exit 1, the error names the endpoint, the seconds, how many listing requests were made and what the last one failed with | A restore against a slow but live endpoint whose listing takes longer than the budget fails where it might have finished; run it again | Operator: check the endpoint, run the restore again | core::`a_spent_listing_budget_stops_the_retries_and_says_what_was_tried`, core::`the_budget_ends_a_retry_that_would_begin_at_its_end`, core::`the_budget_starts_at_the_first_listing`, core::`a_spent_budget_leaves_later_listings_one_attempt_each`, core::`a_batch_listing_is_held_to_the_budget_too`, core::`reads_keep_the_policys_attempts_after_the_budget_is_spent`, core::`a_store_without_a_budget_retries_listings_by_the_policy_alone`, core::`a_budget_that_is_never_spent_reports_nothing`, cli::`a_restore_that_cannot_list_names_the_endpoint_and_what_was_tried`, cli::`a_restore_error_with_the_budget_unspent_is_not_renamed`, cli::`the_restore_listing_budget_is_two_minutes`; plant::`listing-budget-never-spent`, plant::`restore-timeout-not-named` |  |  |  | The daemon's restore-if-missing at start keeps the retry policy alone (it is not a person waiting, and its failure is a reported start state). |  |
