### Handover with Go Litestream

The compat tests are `#[ignore]` in the gate and run by `just compat`, which CI runs on every pull request against the pinned Go build. Every Go 0.5.x release and upstream head run nightly (`compat-releases.yml`). Go 0.5.16 and later reads what this port writes.

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GO-1 | Rust starts on Go's staging directory and replica, WAL intact | continue from Go's newest file. No snapshot | none | None | n/a | compat::`rust_continues_from_every_go_written_newest_file_and_its_next_file_passes_go_rules`, compat::`rust_resumes_from_go_meta`, compat::`go_config_and_meta_start_the_rust_sidecar_with_no_snapshot` |  | compat::`rust_continues_from_every_go_written_newest_file_and_its_next_file_passes_go_rules`, compat::`rust_resumes_from_go_meta`, compat::`go_config_and_meta_start_the_rust_sidecar_with_no_snapshot` |  |  |  |
| GO-2 | Go left staged files not yet uploaded, in the live WAL generation | BOOT-4: read whole, checked and uploaded as staged. A file that does not chain is deleted and staged again from the WAL | none | None | Automatic | compat::`go_and_rust_alternate_on_one_prefix` |  | compat::`go_and_rust_alternate_on_one_prefix` |  |  |  |
| GO-3 | Go's pending files cross a WAL restart | BOOT-4: uploaded as staged, as Go does; the boot's snapshot (`GenerationChanged`) follows | the snapshot's cause | None | Automatic | compat::`go_and_rust_alternate_on_one_prefix` |  | compat::`go_and_rust_alternate_on_one_prefix` |  |  |  |
| GO-4 | Go's newest file holds frames past the committed end (Go through 0.5.18 can replicate frames of a writer stopped mid-commit) | BOOT-9: snapshot, `LocalRewound` | `local_rewound` | The replica holds a transaction SQLite never committed, written by Go. It stays in the history until retention | Automatic for the latest state | compat::`a_tail_past_the_wal_index_is_recognised` |  |  |  |  |  |
| GO-5 | Go's WAL was truncated or restarted since its newest file | BOOT-7 or BOOT-8: snapshot | cause | None | Automatic | compat::`a_go_newest_file_whose_wal_was_truncated_is_a_snapshot_not_a_continue` |  |  |  |  |  |
| GO-6 | Go crossed a WAL restart on its own heuristic and uploaded everything before Rust starts | continue. The replica is trusted as it stands | none | Whatever Go's heuristic missed stays missing. Nothing heals it until the next snapshot, and nothing detects it short of VERIFY-3 | The daily snapshot |  |  |  | untested | **untested** as a hazard; the continue itself is GO-1's evidence |  |
| GO-7 | Go starts on Rust's staging directory and replica | Go continues, by Go's own rules | n/a | Go releases through 0.5.18 can then replicate uncommitted frames (GO-4, from the other side) | Use a Go build with the upstream fix | compat::`go_resumes_from_rust_meta`, compat::`go_resumes_after_rust_checkpoint`, compat::`go_would_continue_at_every_position_of_a_chain_rust_wrote` |  | compat::`go_resumes_from_rust_meta`, compat::`go_resumes_after_rust_checkpoint` |  |  |  |
| GO-8 | Go and Rust alternate on one prefix, with a kill at a random point | Each start is one of the rows above. Every acknowledged transaction restores | cause per boot | None observed | n/a | compat::`go_and_rust_alternate_on_one_prefix`, compat::`go_and_rust_sidecars_alternate_on_one_config_file` |  | compat::`go_and_rust_alternate_on_one_prefix`, compat::`go_and_rust_sidecars_alternate_on_one_config_file` |  |  |  |
| GO-9 | Go and Rust run at the same time on one prefix | UP-10 and UP-14: two writers. Go's PUTs are unconditional and Go takes no meta-directory lock, so a Go process can replace this engine's files and is not refused at start; this engine's own puts are create-only, so it never replaces Go's | `other_writer` in some cycles, late; in one cycle of three the engine logged no cause and shut down cleanly | A Go put replaces one of this engine's files, and Go's compaction merges both writers' level-0 files into a level-1 file: one transaction id has two contents. A later `lsr restore` of a past point or instant exits 0 with a database that fails `PRAGMA integrity_check`; in two exploring runs the latest restore went backwards or was unsound | Operator: stop one writer before the other starts. Nothing in the engine repairs a prefix both have written | `scripts/fault_rig.py` (the Go nemesis: the pinned Go Litestream on the same prefix for 10 seconds, restores judged after each cycle; 2 of 3 cycles in its first run were unsound; the first counted Linux run, nightly 45565674128 on 2026-11-25, had 151 known anomalies in the segments of its 45 Go cycles, 35 of them cycles where the engine logged no OtherWriter, see D216) |  |  | a restore after both writers: known wrong (issue #346) | Found by the fault rig on 2026-11-24 (D195). The rig exempts anomalies in a Go-touched segment under the name KNOWN_GO_ISSUE until #346 closes. The rig is a script and no nextest test, so the citation is its path. Open: a notice for an overwrite of the engine's own file, a restore that checks what it restored, and Go's unconnected bucket lease (D173). See WORLD-5 |  |

### Fail-stop

`if-replication-stops` (`report`, `hold-start` by default, `stop-app`) and `max-unreplicated-age`. Three things trip it and nothing else; what a trip does depends on the mode and on whether the application was released yet. The library refuses commits through a commit hook; `-exec` holds or stops the child; plain sidecar answers 503.

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FS-1 | The engine stops for any cause but the end of a drain (`failstop.rs`, `actor/lag.rs`): with the default responses only another writer; any cause set to `stop` | trip `Stopped(cause)` at once, with no bound; under `hold-start` an application not yet released is held, under `stop-app` a running one is held too (its writes blocked, and an `-exec` child stopped), under `report` nothing is held | `/healthz` 503 with `held_by` the stop's cause name and `app` = `held`, log `start refused` or `app stopped`, and `writes blocked` while the write lock is held | What the stop itself leaves off the replica. While writes are held the replicator keeps the database's write lock, so every writer in any process gets "database is locked"; one transaction already writing when the hold comes may still commit, and a stopped replicator holds nothing (decision D192) | Operator: fix the cause, restart. For another writer's stop (BOOT-22): `lsr trust-local` if this database is the right one, `lsr restore` if the replica is | core::`an_unsafe_stop_trips_and_a_drain_does_not`, core::`a_stop_trips_and_wins`, core::`the_gate_follows_the_table`, core::`the_release_latches`, replica::`an_unsafe_stop_while_running_blocks_writes_only_under_stop_app`, replica::`a_panic_that_stops_the_replicator_keeps_writes_blocked_under_stop_app`, cli::`a_plain_sidecar_blocks_writes_under_stop_app`, sim::`writes_held_after_a_stop`, plant::`gate-dropped-by-a-checkpoint-sim`, cli::`an_unsafe_stop_at_boot_holds_the_child`, cli::`an_unsafe_stop_while_running_stops_the_child_under_stop_app`, plant::`failstop-starts-over-a-stop`, plant::`failstop-exec-ignores-the-gate`, plant::`failstop-stop-app-never-stops` |  |  |  | Another writer's boot stop from #110 is covered by the same trip with no code of its own |  |
| FS-2 | A commit has been off the replica for `max-unreplicated-age` (10 minutes, at least 5), and a sync or upload attempt made at or after the bound failed. The clock is the oldest date of what is off the replica now: a pending file's stamp, or when this process first saw unstaged WAL frames (carried to the first file they went into until the replica holds it), never the start of an unbroken run of lag (decision D85); at the bound the sync and upload waits are let go once, the probe | trip `UnreplicatedTooLong`; held only under `stop-app` (decision D17); clears once the commits it tripped on reach the replica, not only at zero age (decision D86) | `/healthz` 503, `held_by` = `unreplicated_too_long`; `lsr_unreplicated_age_seconds` | Commits newer than the bound, until uploads land. While writes are held the replicator keeps the database's write lock, so every writer in any process gets "database is locked"; one transaction already writing when the hold comes may still commit, and a stopped replicator holds nothing (decision D192) | Automatic once uploads land | core::`a_store_down_past_the_bound_trips_at_the_probe`, core::`a_trip_for_age_clears_once_caught_up`, core::`a_steady_writer_whose_uploads_trail_by_a_tick_never_trips`, core::`failstop::tests::a_trip_for_age_clears_once_its_commits_land`, core::`failstop::tests::staged_frames_never_date_a_newer_file`, core::`failstop::tests::staged_frames_carry_their_date_until_the_replica_holds_them`, sim::`a_store_down_past_the_bound_trips_in_time_and_clears_after`, plant::`failstop-never-trips`, plant::`failstop-no-probe`, plant::`failstop-no-clear`, plant::`failstop-clock-from-last-failure`, plant::`failstop-dates-the-run`, plant::`failstop-carried-outlives-its-file`, plant::`failstop-carries-to-the-newest-file`, plant::`failstop-trip-held-to-zero-age` |  | sim::`a_store_down_past_the_bound_trips_in_time_and_clears_after` |  |  |  |
| FS-3 | The process restarts, or crash-loops, while commits are staged and not uploaded | the clock keeps the oldest pending staged file's stamp, and health's `unreplicated_since` with it | as FS-2 | as FS-2 | Automatic | core::`a_crash_loop_does_not_reset_the_clock`, sim::`a_crash_loop_trips_for_age`, replica::`a_restart_keeps_the_age_of_a_commit_off_the_replica`, plant::`failstop-clock-from-process-start`, plant::`failstop-health-lag-restarts` |  |  |  | A boot that deletes pending files that fail its check stages a snapshot stamped at that boot, so the clock moves once, there; WAL frames no life staged count from the life that sees them (decision D80) |  |
| FS-4 | The engine owes a snapshot for commits that may be in the database file only (a checkpoint past the synced end wrote a TRUNCATE, or a power loss took the WAL and the boot found the database behind what was staged), and a clock was already running | the lag that was running keeps running until the snapshot is staged | as FS-2 | The commits the TRUNCATE moved | Automatic | core::`a_snapshot_owed_after_a_rewind_keeps_the_lag_once_the_replica_has_the_files`, plant::`failstop-forgets-owed-snapshot` |  |  |  | health's own formula read zero in this window before the fix. Only a non-doubt cause counts as owed (`actor/lag.rs`): after D173 the causes that reach it with a clock running are the boot's rewound database and a boundary snapshot the checkpoint could not stage; a failed WAL sync no longer leads to one |  |
| FS-5 | A staged file that changes no page (the driver's start and the frame after each checkpoint rewrite page 1 unchanged), whether a sync step or a checkpoint staged it; or a snapshot for a doubt alone (an operator's request, a WAL whose continuity cannot be proven, a newest staged file unreadable at boot) staged while the WAL held nothing past the staged files before it, whether or not the replica holds those files yet | not lag: no clock, no trip however long the replica refuses it; a boot finds such files among the pending ones while it seeds the page table; while a doubt snapshot is owed with no synced position, WAL frames up to the end seen when the doubt was first observed are not lag either (decision D81) | none | None | n/a | core::`a_file_that_changes_nothing_is_not_lag`, core::`a_file_that_changes_nothing_is_not_lag_after_a_restart`, core::`an_operator_snapshot_while_caught_up_is_not_lag`, core::`an_operator_snapshot_behind_a_pending_commit_is_not_lag`, core::`a_commit_after_a_file_that_changes_nothing_is_lag`, core::`a_doubt_snapshot_at_boot_over_a_replicated_wal_is_not_lag`, core::`a_doubt_at_boot_over_a_replicated_wal_is_not_lag_before_its_snapshot`, core::`a_commit_after_a_doubt_at_boot_is_lag`, sim::`a_checkpoint_file_that_changes_no_page_does_not_trip_after_a_day_long_step`, plant::`failstop-no-op-file-is-lag`, plant::`failstop-no-op-forgotten-at-boot`, plant::`failstop-operator-snapshot-is-lag`, plant::`failstop-quiet-hides-commits`, plant::`failstop-checkpoint-quiet-forgotten`, plant::`failstop-doubt-baseline-ignored`, plant::`failstop-doubt-snapshot-is-lag`, plant::`failstop-quiet-snapshot-needs-caught-up` |  |  |  | Found by the false-trip judge over random histories; the checkpoint's files and the doubt at boot were found later on main (seeds 0x8ef, 0xf4342, 0x14b), and a snapshot staged behind a pending commit later still (D240). Go and lsr both write untracked files, so no checksum in the files says the snapshot equals the replica, and a restore to compare reads the whole database |  |

A refusal of the start's sweep of open uploads for want of permission is not one (BOOT-31) | trip `RefusedAtStart`; holds under `hold-start` and `stop-app` until the first listing, then releases with no operator action (decision D16) | `/healthz` 503, `held_by` = `refused_at_start`; log `writes blocked`; every write fails with "database is locked" (`SQLITE_BUSY`) | None from the hold; commits made meanwhile are local. The trip is kept in health until a restart; nothing panics. Under `keep-trying` the task degrades and the unreplicated-age bound trips it later. Transient faults (local I/O, a full disk, a moved WAL, a lost read transaction, a busy database) are retried | log `writes blocked` with `held_by=<cause>` at WARN; `Health::held_by`, `Health::writes` `Blocked(Tripped(..))`, `Health::phase` `Stopped`; events `WritesBlocked` after `WritesAllowed` | Commits the application made between the release and the stop are in the database and the WAL, not on the replica, until an operator resolves the stop | As the stop's row | replica::`a_stop_the_boot_snapshot_finds_blocks_writes_again_under_stop_app`, cli::`a_plain_sidecar_blocks_writes_under_stop_app` |  |  |  |  |  |
| FS-13 | A boot over an empty meta directory finds the replica's own state and compares the database with the replica's newest state before it decides (the identity check) | writes stay held through the check, which restores the replica's newest state into page checksums and reads every local page: about a restore's time, store bandwidth plus one read of the database (minutes for tens of GiB on S3). The engine announces the check a tick before it runs it (`Event::CheckingIdentity`), and the hold's reason is `checking_identity`, not `booting` (D201) | log `comparing the database with the replica before starting` (INFO, field `tip`), then `writes blocked` with `held_by=checking_identity` (INFO); `Health::writes` `Blocked(CheckingIdentity)` | None: writes wait | Automatic once the check decides | core::`a_new_meta_directory_over_the_replicas_own_state_snapshots_above_it`, sim::`writes_held_while_the_boot_checks_identity`, plant::`failstop-identity-check-unnamed` |  |  |  |  |  |

A run of failed attempts, none by a refusal, that has lasted 15 s (`BOOT_UNDECIDED_BOUND`, counted from the run's first failure) judges it: writes are released and an `-exec` child starts, as Go would have started it. A refusal ends the run and holds until healed (FS-6); a hang holds for the request deadline plus the bound (decision D207) | `writes blocked` with `held_by=booting` until `writes allowed`; `Health::writes` | After a release without a decision, commits are unreplicated until the boot succeeds; if that boot then stops for another writer, the application has already started. Fail-stop's age bound does not run before the first boot (D80) | Automatic once the store answers | core::`a_local_failure_before_the_listing_does_not_release_a_start_another_writer_stops`, core::`a_timeout_at_boot_is_no_refusal`, core::`a_store_down_for_the_bound_judges_the_boot`, core::`a_failing_boot_wakes_at_the_bound`, core::`a_refusal_restarts_the_bound`, sim::`a_store_down_at_boot_does_not_trip`, plant::`boot-judged-by-one-failed-attempt` |  |  |  | Found by a review of what a start does when one attempt fails before the listing. **untested** end to end: no replica or CLI test shows the child starting after the bound |  |
| FS-11 | A checkpoint, the write barrier or a sequence bump comes due while the replicator holds the application's writes | the engine waits (`Fault::WritesHeld`, retried after the retry interval, never counted and never degraded) and checkpoints once writes go through; the held lock survives every engine step, a panic and a rebuild (decision D192) | engine event `WritesHeld`; debug log `checkpoint waits while writes are blocked` | The WAL grows by nothing meanwhile: no one can write | Automatic | core::`writes_held_waits`, replica::`a_checkpoint_waits_while_writes_are_held_and_runs_after`, sqlite::`a_held_gate_blocks_a_writer_nothing_set_up_and_no_reader`, sqlite::`a_set_up_connection_fails_at_once_while_the_gate_holds`, sim::`writes_held_through_a_checkpoint`, oracle::`WritesLeaked`, plant::`gate-never-taken` |  |  |  |  |  |

### Config and startup

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

In the sidecar the other databases keep running | `/healthz` 503, `lsr_db_state` `failed`; non-zero exit at the end | That database is not replicated | Operator |  |  |  | code only | cli::`one_database_failing_to_start_leaves_the_other_replicating`. The cited test uses a file that is not a database, so it shows only that one failing start leaves the others running. The three refusals in this row have no test |  |
| CFG-5 | The same database file (device and inode) is already replicated in this process | refuse, `AlreadyReplicated` | error | None | n/a |  |  |  | code only | **code only** |  |
| CFG-6 | The database file does not exist yet | The sidecar waits and checks again every `monitor-interval` | `lsr_db_state` `waiting`; `/healthz` 200 | n/a | Automatic when the file appears | cli::`a_database_created_after_start_is_picked_up` |  |  |  |  |  |
| CFG-7 | Credentials from a source that fetches them: web identity (`AWS_WEB_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE` or a profile's `web_identity_token_file`), a profile's `credential_process`, container credentials (`AWS_CONTAINER_CREDENTIALS_RELATIVE_URI` or `_FULL_URI`), the EC2 instance metadata service | continue with the first source that answers, in the order of Go's SDK. A source that fails to answer at start (a refusal, a body that holds no credentials, a token file that cannot be read, an unreachable or erroring endpoint, a container URL that is not loopback or a container address, the metadata service failing when nothing else is configured) does not refuse the start: the store opens and asks the chain again with each request, as Go's SDK fetches credentials per request, and the log says `credentials not available yet, retrying` with the source's words. Each request meanwhile fails without reaching the bucket: `Transient` when waiting may fix it (no answer, 5xx, 429), as a credentials refusal otherwise; the engine retries as for any store refusal, and the first request after the source answers signs and goes. A configuration no source can satisfy (nothing named and the metadata service switched off, a profile that cannot be used, a shared file that cannot be read) refuses the start naming the source, since asking again cannot change it. The metadata service is the source reached when nothing is configured, so its failure is reported in the list of every source tried | `credentials not available yet, retrying` in the log at start; then `/healthz` and health name the failure as for any store refusal (`credentials`, or retrying and then stalled for a transient one); an error at start naming the source for a configuration no source can satisfy | Under the default `if-replication-stops: hold-start`, writes are held until the boot is judged. A source that does not answer or answers 5xx fails the boot transiently, so writes are released after 15 s of failing (`BOOT_UNDECIDED_BOUND`), as for any store that is down. A source that refuses (a 403, a rejected token) is a credentials refusal, which holds until healed (FS-6); `report` does not hold. Nothing is lost: commits are staged and uploaded once the credentials come | Automatic when the source answers, with no restart; operator for a configuration no source can satisfy | compat::`go_and_rust_fetch_the_same_temporary_credentials`, store-s3::`instance_metadata_falls_back_without_a_token`, store-s3::`container_host_must_be_loopback_or_a_container_address`, store-s3::`credential_process_output_is_checked`, store-s3::`environment_keys_come_before_web_identity_and_web_identity_before_the_host`, store-s3::`only_a_source_that_failed_to_answer_is_a_fetch_failure`, store-s3::`a_chain_that_cannot_be_reached_fails_each_request_transient_and_sends_nothing`, store-s3::`a_chain_the_service_refuses_is_a_credentials_refusal_and_sends_nothing`, store-s3::`the_store_signs_once_the_chain_answers_and_asks_no_more`, config::`a_source_that_is_down_at_start_opens_a_store_that_asks_again`, config::`a_configuration_that_cannot_yield_credentials_is_refused`; plant::`imds-token-fallback-narrow`, plant::`container-host-unchecked`, plant::`process-version-unchecked`, plant::`web-identity-before-env-keys`, plant::`credential-failure-at-open-refuses-the-start`, plant::`fetch-error-ignores-the-metadata-look` |  |  |  | Before, the start was refused for any source failure (this row said "as Go", which was wrong: Go's `Init` loads the SDK config without fetching, and the SDK fetches on the first request), so a pod that started before its credential agent answered, or an instance metadata service answering 5xx once, left that database unreplicated, with no replicator and no hold, until someone restarted the daemon. Not tested end to end through the daemon: the process environment cannot be set under the suite, so the store's and the open function's own tests carry it. Built for #176 (D44). The compat test is a differential against Go's SDK through the dumper, both pointed at one fake STS, container endpoint and metadata service by the SDK's endpoint variables, over 1024 generated cases a run. It found two differences, fixed toward Go: the metadata token now falls back to asking without one on any failure but a 400, and a body that is not JSON is malformed before its code is read. Server errors (5xx) are not generated, since Go retries them with backoff; their class is a store-s3 unit test |  |

### Circumstances the code does not distinguish

These have no enum variant. WORLD-1 to WORLD-31 came from the first review and WORLD-32 to WORLD-40 from the second; none was found by the author unprompted, which says the next reader will find more. The response column is a reading of what the existing rows imply. Where the mark is **unknown**, the response is a guess and is labelled as one. The first five matter most.

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WORLD-1 | Two processes replicate one database through one staging directory (an overlapping rolling deploy; `lsr replicate` run by hand beside the daemon; two databases configured with one `meta_dir`) | **refuse** at start (`lsr replicate` waits for the lock and starts once the holder exits, docs/decisions/D178.md): `Replicator::start` takes an exclusive `flock` on `<meta>/lsr-lock` for the thread's life, and a second engine in this or another process on the host gets `MetaLocked` (CFG-36). The lock is on a file of its own, never replaced, so SQLite closing other descriptors cannot drop it. It excludes on local filesystems (ext4, XFS, Btrfs, ZFS, tmpfs; APFS, HFS+). Not covered: NFS, SMB and FUSE mounts, where a lock may be emulated or local to one client, and a Go process, which takes no lock; there the old outcome stands (both stage `confirmed + 1` under one key, and the other-writer check compares the replica with the shared staging, so it probably cannot fire) | error | Outside the lock's reach: one key written twice with possibly different page sets. Outcome for G1: **unknown** | Automatic inside the lock's reach (the second waits); outside it, the operator stops the other engine | replica::`second_writer::a_second_engine_on_one_meta_directory_is_refused`, replica::`second_writer::a_second_process_on_one_meta_directory_is_refused`, sidecar::`daemon::tests::a_start_on_a_locked_meta_directory_waits_for_the_lock`; plant::`meta-dir-unlocked` |  |  | outside the lock's reach: unknown | The lock's reach is by reading the platforms' `flock` semantics; only Linux local disk and tmpfs are exercised | #96 would make the second PUT of a level-0 key fail outside the lock's reach |
| WORLD-2 | The process is paused while it holds the write barrier (CKPT-1) or only its read transaction | The barrier is an open write transaction. While paused, every application writer blocks and then gets `SQLITE_BUSY` at its `busy_timeout`; under the read transaction alone the WAL cannot be recycled and grows. On resume the wall clock has jumped forward (G3), every in-flight store request has passed its stall bound, 60 s (UP-2), and a multipart upload may have expired | None while paused: the process cannot report. `reported_at` in `Health` goes stale; `lsr_health_report_age_seconds` shows it only if something else scrapes | Application availability for the length of the pause. No replica damage expected. Guess | Automatic on resume. Guess |  |  |  | unknown | None |  |
| WORLD-3 | Database, WAL and staging are put back together from an older VM or volume snapshot; the bucket is not | BOOT-22: the replica holds newer files the WAL cannot vouch for and no takeover record covers, so the boot stops, `OtherWriter`, and writes nothing. If the old state is the right one, `lsr trust-local <db> --replica <url>` lets the next start snapshot it above the replica's newest transaction (BOOT-6). If the replica is the right one, `lsr restore` gets the newer state | `other_writer`, the refusal log line; after `lsr trust-local`, `behind_remote` | None until the operator chooses. After `lsr trust-local` the latest state goes back to the snapshot's state (G2 exception, asked for). The newer history stays in the bucket until retention removes it, restorable by id | Operator | sim::`vm_restore_stops_until_trust_local`, core::`vouch::a_vm_restore_stops_until_a_takeover_covers_the_replica`; plant::`boot-gate-takeover-ignored` |  | sim::`vm_restore_stops_until_trust_local` |  | The scenario saves the meta directory, commits on, puts the meta directory back, truncates the WAL and commits again: the database and WAL are the simulator's, so they are not put back byte for byte |  |
| WORLD-4 | A restore lists the replica while files change under it | `restore` lists and plans from that one listing. A file that vanishes is replanned (REST-4). A level-0 key overwritten mid-read (BOOT-16) is read as whatever bytes arrive; a mixed read fails decode. A listing that lags the bucket is not handled: such a store is unsupported (D173) | none | On a store that lags, a restore can return an older state as `Latest` with success. A store that passes the consistency probe does not | Run again | core::`a_file_deleted_between_plan_and_read_is_replanned_once`, core::`files_vanishing_on_every_attempt_are_unstable` |  |  |  | The restore's read past the listing, its chase of a lagging listing (`ListingBehind`, exit 3) and the simulator's lagging-listing hold were removed on purpose (D173) |  |
| WORLD-5 | Failover: a new host restores and starts while the old host is still running | When both hosts are litestream-rs on a store with conditional writes, both put create-only level-0 files (UP-14), so the second put of each id is refused. The refused host's relist finds the other's verifying file at its next id while its staged copy is its own, and stops with `OtherWriter` (UP-17), keeping the stop across restarts (BOOT-22). The other host goes on | `other_writer` on one side | None of this engine's files is replaced, and the stopped host's commits after the stop are unreplicated. With Go on either side, or a store without conditional writes, nothing is refused: one transaction can end with two contents, and a restore can return the wrong one without an error (GO-9, UP-18) | Operator: fence the old host before starting the new one | core::`failover_with_the_old_host_alive_never_publishes_one_id_twice` |  |  |  | The test drives the old host and the new host on one replica and asserts that no id is published twice and that one host stops. |  |
| WORLD-6 | The process dies part way through deleting "the file and every staged file after it" (BOOT-5, BOOT-11, BOOT-13, RUN-16) | `drop_staged_from` deletes newest first, one at a time, so a crash leaves the oldest of them. ### The checkers themselves

A checker that cannot see a class of bug reads as green. These rows are the checkers' own blind spots that were closed, each backed by a planted bug that its test kills, and the one that stays open. The response is what the checker does.

| ID | Circumstance and how it is detected | Response | Signal | Data at risk, and the bound | Recovery | Detected by | Survived by | Recovered by | No-test mark | Notes | In review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CHK-1 | A transaction id covers a second commit after a power loss that cut neither commit | The oracle fails the run: an id may cover a second commit only if the replica lost the first from outside, or a power loss cut the first before the second was put | oracle `TxidReused` | None (a checker) | None | sim::`oracle::tests::a_power_loss_that_cut_nothing_covered_does_not_excuse_a_reused_id`; plant::`oracle-reuse-after-any-power-loss` |  |  |  | Before, any power loss or rollback in a run turned the reuse check off for the rest of it |  |
| CHK-2 | Latest falls below an acknowledged transaction after a power loss, which never touches the replica | The oracle fails the run: acknowledgements survive power loss; a bucket rollback voids those from the lowest id it uncovered | oracle `LatestLostAck` | None (a checker) | None | sim::`world::tests::a_power_loss_keeps_the_acknowledgements`; plant::`acks-forgotten-on-power-loss` |  |  |  |  |  |
| CHK-3 | Latest goes back over a recorded rewind the engine never reported | The history judge fails the run: a rewind explains a regression only after the engine reported a loss (a loss-reporting snapshot cause, a first sync over files, or a stop or degraded state that names one) | judge `Unsignaled` | None (a checker) | None | history::`judge::a_rewind_the_engine_did_not_report_does_not_excuse_going_back`, history::`judge::a_report_before_the_rewind_does_not_cover_it`, history::`judge::a_report_after_any_explaining_rewind_covers_the_step`, sim::`run::tests::a_first_sync_over_files_reports_a_loss`; plant::`rewind-excused-without-report`, plant::`rewind-report-only-after-the-latest`, plant::`first-sync-never-a-report` |  |  |  |  |  |
| CHK-4 | A regression the driver excused (a bucket rollback) is never reported by the engine | Counted (`history.excused.unreported`), not fatal: only a listing that ends below what the engine confirmed raises `RemoteRegressed` (D17) | None | Latest goes back with no signal | None |  |  |  | accepted, undetected | Scenarios and recorded histories only: the generators no longer draw a rollback (D173) |  |
| CHK-5 | A replica key is put again with other bytes while every transaction id keeps its commit | The oracle fails the run, even after a delete, unless a scoped exemption of CHK-1 applies | oracle `KeyRewritten` | None (a checker) | None | sim::`oracle::tests::a_key_put_again_with_other_bytes_is_a_rewrite`; plant::`keys-not-write-once` |  |  |  | Catches a compacted key rewritten under the same commit, which `TxidReused` cannot see |  |
| CHK-6 | A restore gives the database as a flipped bit in the WAL left it, a state the world adopted and no commit made | The history judge fails the run | oracle `CorruptionRestored` | None (a checker) | None | sim::`history::tests::a_restore_of_a_state_adopted_from_damage_fails`; plant::`damage-adopted-as-commit` |  |  |  | Before, the adopted state counted as a commit, so a replica holding it passed |  |
| CHK-7 | A task stays stalled on a retried fault after every injected fault has healed | The oracle fails the run at the end of the final drain or after idle's settle window, unless the world still imposes the stall's fault (an exhaustive excuse table, empty today) | oracle `StuckRetrying` | None (a checker) | None | core::`degraded::a_retried_fault_that_persists_ends_degraded_as_a_stall`, sim::`stall_liveness::a_stall_ends_with_the_fault_that_caused_it`; oracle::`StuckRetrying`, plant::`success-keeps-a-stall` |  |  |  | Before, any stall ended a liveness judgment and excused an undrained shutdown, so an engine still retrying a fault nothing caused any more, or reporting degraded for nothing, passed. The run's end now also heals the SQLite source failures still queued |  |
| CHK-8 | An error the engine's SQLite seam or one of its stores can return, from some call, that no simulated history ever draws | The simulator's error domain (`sim/src/domain.rs`) lists every SQLite-side operation against every source error, and every store operation on both stores against every store error and denial kind, in matches with no wildcard: each cell is drawn, a world fault, impossible with a reason checked against lsr-sqlite, store-fs and store-s3, or unmodelled. A new operation or error variant breaks the build there | None (a checker) | None (a checker) | None | sim::`domain::tests::every_cell_is_drawn_or_says_why_not`, sim::`domain::tests::the_source_draw_reaches_every_drawable_cell_and_no_other`, sim::`domain::tests::the_store_draw_reaches_every_drawable_class_and_no_other` |  |  |  | Before, histories drew two store errors and two SQLite errors on nine of twelve operations. The whole domain (now 22 source cells and 41 store cells drawn, 4 source cells unmodelled; drawn last in the swarm mix) found a defect in compaction classifying staging errors as the replica's (1 seed, fixed in #145, its seed must pass). A failed WAL sync is not drawn: D173 makes it a scenario of its own, since the engine goes on after it and the simulator's model of SQLite cannot follow what the kernel then drops. Other unmodelled cells are gaps in the simulator listed in section 6 (C9): an unlinked WAL. A checkpoint whose read-transaction retake is lost is drawn: the simulated checkpointer ends and retakes the read transaction around a failed checkpoint as lsr-sqlite does. WAL damage while a failed sync's writeback is lost is skipped and counted (`wal.damage_skipped.writeback_lost`) |  |
| CHK-9 | A lasting fault overlaps a crash, a power loss, a shutdown or another fault, or lasts long enough to stall; every store operation is down at once; another writer's file or a regrown WAL stands while the replica is judged | Under the overlap draw (drawn last in the swarm mix), a hold lasts up to 200 ticks and each of its steps is drawn: a commit, a tick, a one-shot store fault from the error domain, and a process crash, a power loss or a shutdown with the fault still in place. The holds every store operation down at once, another writer's file, and the WAL regrowths are drawn too, and judged while they hold | None (a checker) | None (a checker) | None | sim::`survival::a_store_down_for_every_operation_at_once_leaves_restorable_files`, sim::`survival::a_regrown_wal_leaves_the_replica_restorable_while_it_lasts`, sim::`survival::a_second_writer_s_file_leaves_the_replica_restorable_while_it_stands` |  |  |  | Before, a hold ran at most 11 ticks of commits and ticks, with no restart or second fault inside, and never held every store operation, a second writer or a regrown WAL. The overlap draw found that a halt outlasting a restart goes unreported (46 of 3000 mixed seeds, first at seed 233), which D174 accepts as Go's behaviour; the simulator no longer judges it. It also found that the oracle mapped a standing second writer's id to an older put (fixed), that the stall check judged tasks a drain never runs (fixed), that the read budget did not count a degraded task's retries (fixed), that the retry-wait check measured from the wall clock while the engine's time slews after a step back (fixed), and passed on restores through a long stale listing (0x7f8, 0x12e) and an acknowledged level-0 object deleted after its key was put again (0xb6f, the reused-id class) to their owners |  |
| CHK-10 | An obligation a fault leaves the engine owing is held only in memory, so a restart right after the fault forgets it | Under the kill-after-fault law (drawn last in the swarm mix, half of it), the process dies right after the first tick in which an injected store fault or SQLite-side failure fires after a fault operation, once per operation. Whatever the fault left the engine owing must then be found again by the restarted engine, so an obligation held only in memory fails the run | None (a checker) | None (a checker) | None | sim::`kill_after_fault::half_the_swarm_draws_the_law_and_the_default_mix_never` |  |  |  | The law showed the second-writer judge charging a stop to a process it had just killed (fixed: a crash ends the watch, as a boot does), and a lost acknowledged transaction on seed 0x1dc, under triage. The engine journal and its obligation ledger, which the law once proved, are gone (D173) |  |
---

## 4. Snapshot causes

<!-- enum: SnapshotCause -->

Every `SnapshotCause` variant (`core/src/fault.rs`). A snapshot reads every page of the database under the read transaction and uploads it as one level-0 file; each compaction level later rewrites it. That every variant has a simulator scenario is asserted by sim `every_cause_has_a_scenario` and `every_scenario_runs`.

"Cheaper proof" is the author's analysis; none of the cheaper proofs is built, and none is in the repository as a design. "Needed" asks whether the doubt behind the snapshot is real.

| `SnapshotCause` | Fires when | Why a snapshot | Needed? | Cheaper sound proof | Rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FirstSync` | No staged file and no replica level-0 file to continue from | There is nothing to build on | Yes | None | BOOT-1 |
| `BehindRemote` | At boot, the replica's newest transaction is above the newest staged file's, the WAL does not vouch for the replica's newest file, and a takeover record covers it | An operator trusted this database over a replica it has no record of writing (`lsr trust-local`), or a restore recorded the takeover | The doubt is real | None: the boot already reads the replica's newest level-0 file and continues without a snapshot when the WAL vouches for it | BOOT-6, BOOT-22 |
| `NewestStagedFileUnreadable` | A staged file failed verification twice, or diverged from the replica's object, or had no token (boot refuses no more than the cap: RUN-18 holds the position instead) | The chain from the replica to the WAL has a hole in staging | After a divergence, yes. After a drop for verification it is taken only when recovery cannot continue from the newest file left (BOOT-5); mid-run (RUN-16) it is always taken, since a checkpoint may have removed the dropped frames and nothing judges that before the drop | Mid-run, judge the WAL against the newest file left before dropping, as boot does. The boot drop and the token-cap case (RUN-18) no longer snapshot | BOOT-5, BOOT-13, RUN-16, RUN-17 |
| `WalMissing` | No WAL file, or a torn header | Commits after the position may have been checkpointed and are in no WAL | Yes. Not needed in one common case: a database just restored from this replica, which is snapshotted in full right after being downloaded | Compare the database's pages with per-page digests of the replica's tip and ship only the pages that differ. Needs local state that survives restarts | BOOT-7, RUN-9 |
| `GenerationChanged` | At boot, any salt change. Running, a restart that the read lock and `salt1 + 1` do not prove, or frames abandoned after the position | Commits written after the position and overwritten by the restart are in no WAL | The doubt is real in the sidecar case. Not when the crash fell between the engine's own checkpoint and its first file of the new generation | The page-digest compare above. This is the cause behind the measured crash-loop storm (BOOT-20) | BOOT-8, RUN-8, RUN-10 |
| `PrevFrameMismatch` | The frame before the position is not a commit frame, or the position is mid-transaction | The WAL is not the one the staged file came from | Yes | Page-digest compare | BOOT-10 |
| `LastPageMismatch` | The last page the staged file covers differs from the WAL | Same | Yes | Page-digest compare | BOOT-10 |
| `LocalRewound` | Same generation; the committed end or the file is shorter than the staged end, or the range was rewritten | The replica is ahead of the database, or holds other content. The snapshot puts the replica's latest state back in step | Yes | Page-digest compare ships only the reverted pages | BOOT-9, GO-4 |
| `CheckpointBoundary` | The engine's own TRUNCATE changed the WAL | A commit may have slipped between the last sync and the checkpoint | Usually not: nothing slipped in | A TRUNCATE that cannot swallow a commit (the engine's read on mark 0 blocks every backfill, and a TRUNCATE run from the writer connection after the seal fails busy if anything was committed since), so no snapshot is needed: issue #334 | CKPT-4 |
| `OperatorRequested` | An operator asked | By definition | Yes | None | RUN-23 |
| `StagedUnverified` | At boot, pending staged files that do not read whole or do not chain, and recovery cannot continue | The files cannot be uploaded, so they are not published; the snapshot holds their end state | Only for torn or misplaced files: a plain crash, a WAL restart or a staging copy put back leaves files that read whole and chain, which are uploaded as staged (D175) | None needed | BOOT-11, GO-3 |

**Cost today.** A snapshot costs one full read of the database, one full-size upload, its staged copy, and its share of each later merge. No rate limit, budget or backoff bounds how often any cause may fire. On main there is no per-cause counter, so the rate cannot be alerted on (#86 adds one).

---

## 5. Stop conditions

<!-- enum: StopCause -->

Every `StopCause` variant (`core/src/fault.rs`). A stopped engine does no I/O; every later tick returns the same cause (core `stop_is_terminal`). `/healthz` answers 503 and `lsr_stopped` is set. Nothing but a restart of the replicator clears a stop, and a restart forgets the stop (BOOT-21).

Under the default responses only two causes stop the engine: `OtherWriter` and `Shutdown`. The other twelve stop only when configured to (`Responses::STRICT`, or one field set to `Stop`).

| `StopCause` | Stops by default? | Why continuing is unsafe | What the operator does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `OtherWriter` | **Yes** | Two writers on one prefix give one transaction id two contents, and each one's retention can delete the other's files. Continuing would damage a history this engine does not own | Find the other replicator. Stop one. If this database is the right one, run `lsr trust-local <db> --replica <url>` (or remove the other's files) and start again. If the replica is the right one, restore from it with `lsr restore`. A restart alone stops again (BOOT-21, BOOT-22) |
| `Shutdown` | **Yes**, not configurable | A drain's deadline passed. The caller asked for an end | Nothing. The next start replicates what was left; the stop reported how much |
| `StoreDenied(Denied)` | No | Not unsafe. Stopping makes an access failure loud for an orchestrator | Fix what the kind names (the keys, the policy, the host clock, the bucket name, the TLS trust); restart |
| `StoreRefused` | No | Not unsafe | Read the status in the log; fix the request's cause; restart |
| `RemoteCorrupt` | No | Building on a corrupt object would make files that depend on it | Remove or replace the object named in the log; restart. A snapshot follows if the chain is broken |
| `RemoteRegressed` | No | The replica lost files it had confirmed. Continuing accepts that loss and may reuse the vacated range | Decide whether the rollback was intended. Restart to continue from what the replica holds |
| `WalCorrupt` | No | Nothing past the bad frame can be trusted | Run `PRAGMA integrity_check`; checkpoint from the application so the WAL restarts; restart. A snapshot follows |
| `WalTruncated` | No | Same | Same |
| `WalUnlinked` | No | The file the engine reads is no longer the database's WAL | Restart the application's connections and the replicator |
| `WalSyncFailed` | No | The frames the failed sync covered may never reach the disk | None required: the engine runs the fsync again and goes on, and a power loss before one succeeds makes the next boot snapshot. Under `STRICT` the stop happens once; restart |
| `PageSizeChanged` | No | A replica prefix holds one page size, in this port and in Go | Point the replicator at a new prefix. The old prefix keeps every state up to the change |
| `DatabasePageMismatch` | No | The database file holds bytes no WAL frame wrote. Shipping them replicates possible corruption | Inspect the page named in `/healthz`. Then either repair the database, or accept it (`accept_database_pages`) |
| `Bug` | No | The engine's own state contradicted itself | Report it with the `BugSite` from the log. A restart boots from disk and bucket |

Three other ways replication ends that are not a `StopCause`:

| Condition | Signal | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| The replicator thread is gone, or this process is a fork | `Phase::Gone(ThreadDied | Forked)`, `/healthz` 503 | RUN-21 |
| The database never started | `lsr_db_state` `failed`, `/healthz` 503 | CFG-4 |
| A retry that never succeeds (disk full, long readers) | "retrying", then from the 8th failure in a row a degraded stall naming the fault. No stop; `/healthz` 200 | RUN-13, RUN-14, CKPT-3 |

---

## 6. Known gaps

### Open defects

| # | Defect | Rows | Tracking |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | A crash loop takes one full snapshot per boot with no ceiling; maintenance and retention never run while processes die young; scratch files and open multipart uploads accumulate | BOOT-20, UP-15, MAINT-13 | #97 fixes scratch files and maintenance starvation. The snapshot per boot has no branch |
| D2 | **Accepted limit** (`docs/decisions/D173.md`). A failed WAL fsync is not remembered across a restart: the engine goes on as Go does, and a power loss before a later fsync succeeds leaves the database behind what was staged, which the next boot snapshots | RUN-3, BOOT-21 | None open. Issue #99 |
| D3 | A bucket rolled back while the engine is down is taken as the bucket; freed transaction ids are reused. Accepted as a documented limit (`docs/decisions/D173.md`) | BOOT-19, G1 | None open. Issue #99 |
| D4 | **Closed by #98.** A restore as of an instant could return a state committed up to one tick's read after the instant | G3 | Merged |
| D5 | **Closed by scope.** A boot or relist listing that lags the bucket is trusted; a store that lags is unsupported (D173) | BOOT-18, MAINT-12 | `docs/decisions/D173.md` |
| D6 | **Closed.** Level-0 writes are create-only, so a second writer or a reused id is refused, and UP-17 stops the refused writer (core `failover_with_the_old_host_alive_never_publishes_one_id_twice`, run in the gate). Open: a Go process's puts are unconditional (GO-9) | UP-14, GO-9, WORLD-5 | Merged. GO-9: issue #346 |
| D7 | A retried multipart Complete that already succeeded is reported as `store_refused` | UP-13 | #96 |
| D9 | **Accepted limit** (`docs/decisions/D174.md`). Retry counts, waits and degraded states reset on restart, as Go's do. The one stop the engine makes by default, `OtherWriter`, holds across a restart because every boot refuses the other writer's files again (BOOT-13, BOOT-22), whether it saw them before or was down when they appeared | BOOT-21, BOOT-22; a halt across restarts goes unreported, reached by the simulator's overlapping holds (CHK-9), which count it and do not judge it | `claude/second-writer` for the stop; `docs/decisions/D174.md` for the rest, left out of v1 |
| D10 | A commit is dated by when it was staged. After an outage, a restore as of an instant inside the outage leaves out commits made before that instant. The contract "what the replica held at t" is not stated anywhere a user reads | G3 | None. None |
| D12 | **Closed by #104.** A fault whose policy is a plain retry never became a degraded state. Now each degrades its task as `Stalled(<fault>)` at the 8th failure in a row, and a failed WAL fsync is its own persistent cause, `WalSyncFailed` | RUN-3, RUN-13, RUN-14, CKPT-3 | Merged |
| D13 | **Closed.** Past 4096 pending files a staged file got no token and was certain to be refused, which cost a snapshot and every restore point from there on. The engine now holds its position at the cap, and boot witnesses no more than the cap and stages the rest again from the WAL | RUN-18 | Closed |
| D14 | **Closed.** A boot that dropped an unreadable staged file snapshotted even when the WAL could regenerate it. It now continues when recovery does. The running twin (RUN-16) still snapshots | BOOT-5 | Closed |
| D15 | A staged snapshot of a rewound database is deleted by the next boot if the process died before uploading it, and taken again | BOOT-9 | #89 |
| D16 | **Closed.** A missing bucket and a TLS failure were reported as `credentials`. Each is now its own kind (N4) | UP-4 | Closed |
| D17 | **Decided (D173).** A bucket rolled back while the engine runs is recognised as `remote_regressed` when a listing ends below what the engine confirmed: the uploader's, or maintenance's. A hole below the newest file is not watched; restore and the scheduled verify report it | UP-11, G2 | `claude/second-writer` |

### Defects found by the review of version 1

| # | Defect | Basis | Rows | Tracking |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | **Half closed.** `lsr_unreplicated_age_seconds` read 0 until the first boot of a process completed; it now counts from the start of any wait no booted engine vouches for (`Progress::unproven`, `replica/src/thread.rs`). Open: it restarts from the process's start after every restart, so a crash loop understates lag | replica `the_lag_counts_from_the_start_while_the_first_boot_fails`; plant `lag-zero-before-boot`. The restart half is read in the code, not run | G4, G7 | Issue #99 for the restart half |
| N2 | **Closed.** A backward clock step held every timer for the size of the step: `Actor::clock` returned the old reading while the clock was behind. Observed before the fix (a one-second retry asked to wake an hour later after an hour's step back). Engine time now slews (`actor/clock.rs`) | core `a_backward_clock_step_does_not_freeze_a_retry_wait`, sim `clock_steps_back_an_hour_mid_retry`; plants `clock-step-back-freezes-timers`, `clock-step-back-freezes-timers-sim` | RUN-19, WORLD-14 | Closed, the replica thread's own waits included (WORLD-14) |
| N3 | **Closed on local filesystems.** No cross-process exclusion on the staging directory existed; now an exclusive lock on `<meta>/lsr-lock` refuses a second engine at start (`lsr replicate` waits for it, docs/decisions/D178.md). Open: NFS, SMB and FUSE mounts, and a Go process on the same meta directory | Lock tests in `replica/tests/replica/second_writer.rs` | WORLD-1, A4 | `claude/second-writer` |
| N4 | **Closed.** The `credentials` cause absorbed every 401 and 403, every S3 auth code including `RequestTimeTooSkewed`, a TLS failure and `NoSuchBucket`. Now five kinds (`StoreDenied`), an archived object is `store_refused`, and a batch delete refusing a key is `permission_denied` instead of transient. KMS key codes are `permission_denied` (store-s3 `classify_denials_by_what_to_fix`) | store-s3 `classify_denials_by_what_to_fix`; plant `denial-classes` | UP-4, WORLD-15, WORLD-22, WORLD-23 | Closed |

### Defects found by restores judged during a fault

Found on branch `claude/survival-tests` by the simulator's holds (`Op::Hold`, `sim/src/run/hold.rs`), which judge restores while a fault still holds.

| # | Defect | Rows | Tracking |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Fixed on the branch.** A restore of `Latest` or `AsOf` through a listing that lags published an older database and reported success | WORLD-4, G0, G2 | `claude/survival-tests`; the read-past that fixed it was removed with the lagging-listing cut (D173) |
| S2 | **Fixed on the branch.** A restore over a listing that shows nothing answered "empty replica", which `--if-replica-exists` turns into a skipped restore with success: an application could start on an empty database over a replica that holds its history | WORLD-4, G0 | `claude/survival-tests`; plant `empty-listing-is-empty-replica` |
| S3 | **Fixed on the branch.** A boot with staging gone took a replica header that did not decode for no page size and snapshotted a new page size onto the prefix (swarm seed 0x13, under lying reads) | BOOT-15 | `claude/survival-tests`; plant `replica-page-size-unread` |
| S4 | **Closed** on `claude/second-writer`: the boot refuses replica files nothing vouches for (BOOT-22), and the test runs un-ignored. Swarm seed 0xb9, shrunk to 13 operations (two second writers, a corrupt publish, a checksum-mismatch upload, redone commits): `Latest` goes back from one node to an older one with no rewind recorded, while the oracle passes. Matches the class of D9 (a boot that snapshots over another writer's files) | BOOT-22, G2 | Handed to the second-writer work |
| S5 | **A checker defect, fixed on `claude/survival-followup`.** Swarm seed 0x70f: the compaction byte budget counted a rolled-back level-1 file beside the file that replaced it at that level, 142 bytes over. No engine cost | MAINT | sim `a_rolled_back_output_replaced_at_its_level_counts_once` |
| S6 | Lasting ENOSPC on staging (a transient `StorageFull`, as the directory store reports it) is retried as `LocalIo`, not `LocalDiskFull`; only `ScratchExhausted` maps to `LocalDiskFull` | RUN-14, D12 | Handed to the health and classification work |

### Blind spots in the checkers

These are defects in the evidence, not in the engine. Each weakens a guarantee's claim to be checked.

| # | Blind spot | Effect | Tracking |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Oracle `TxidReused` and the increasing-cover check are disabled for the whole run once any rewind, bucket rollback or lost file has occurred (`covers` in `sim/src/oracle.rs`: `rewound` is true if any snapshot note is rewound, the model rewound, or any file was lost) | The runs where id reuse happens (BOOT-18, BOOT-19) are the runs where the check is off. G1's simulator evidence does not cover id reuse after a rewind | Fixed: scoped to the commits a power loss cut or the replica lost (CHK-1) |
| C2 | Oracle `LatestLostAck` forgets every acknowledgement on every power loss, honest disk or not (`note_rewound`, called from the power-loss path in `sim/src/world.rs`), and on every rollback | G2's simulator evidence cannot fail on a regression after any power loss. The history judge still applies, by content | Fixed: acknowledgements survive power loss (CHK-2) |
| C3 | The judge excuses a regression on the driver's record (`history.power_loss`, `excuse_regression`), never on the engine's signal | "Reported" in G2 was unverified | Fixed in the simulator: judge `Unsignaled` (CHK-3); driver excuses counted when unpaid (CHK-4) |
| C4 | No fault shape holds until a later step; scenarios inject one error and judge after recovery | No row for a lasting store or disk condition earns S | None. Fix: `FaultShape::Until(step)` and restores during the fault |
| C5 | The simulator's oracle restores over an unfaulted store handle | Restore-side faults (WORLD-4) are never judged | None |
| C6 | The real-engine history runs in the gate are one seed with no faults, and one seed of four power-loss cycles; the larger runs are `#[ignore]` | G1's outside-the-engine evidence is thin except nightly LazyFS | None |
| C8 | After WAL or page damage the simulator's model adopts whatever SQLite now reads (`adopt_local_state` in `sim/src/world.rs`) | The oracle could not fail on shipped damage | Fixed: oracle `CorruptionRestored` refuses the adopted state (CHK-6) |
| C7 | The simulator has one clock read once per tick | It could not produce the stamp-order defect #98 fixed | Fixed: a per-read clock law, drawn last in the mix (RUN-25, RUN-26) |
| C9 | The simulator's model cannot follow an unlinked WAL through a later power loss, or WAL damage or a lying disk while a failed sync's writeback is lost | Those error-domain cells are unmodelled and never drawn (CHK-8); the damage and the lying disk are skipped and counted | Issue #127 item D |

### Unknowns

| # | Unknown | What would settle it |
|---|---|---|
| D8 | What SQLite does after a failed fsync of the database file inside a checkpoint, and whether the engine's retry is then safe | A fault VFS that fails `xSync` on the database once during TRUNCATE, then a power cut, compared with SQLite's recovery |
| D11 | **Closed on `claude/staged-delete-durable`.** A staged file deleted without a directory fsync could return after power loss. The directory store now syncs each level directory after a delete, so it cannot (store-fs `a_crash_after_a_delete_never_brings_the_file_back`, plant `delete-without-dir-sync`); whether the engine would have tolerated it is moot. Cost measured: about 0.14 ms per delete call on ext4 | Closed |
| U1 | The outcome of a failed write-barrier release on real SQLite | CKPT-6. A fault VFS run |
| U2 | Behaviour on filesystems where POSIX locks or shared mmap are unreliable | A2. A startup self-test, or a documented refusal |
| U3 | Whether a directory fsync that fails after the rename, then succeeds on retry, can leave a staged file that vanishes later | Not modelled |
| U4 | Compaction correctness beyond synthetic inputs. core `restoring_the_snapshot_and_the_chain_equals_restoring_the_compacted_file` compares the output's pages with `expected_pages`, a last-writer-wins merge written in the test, which is an independent apply; the simulator's oracle also judges restores through merged levels. Not covered: inputs written by SQLite at scale, a decoder not shared with the engine, a differential against Go's compactor | A differential run against Go's compactor on Go-written inputs |
| U9 | WORLD-1, WORLD-2, WORLD-11, WORLD-24: outcomes guessed from reading | A rig that drives the real binary with a second process, pauses, an emptied prefix and a versioned bucket |
| U5 | How stale listings and reads are on Tigris, from a second region, and on R2 under long load | 1000 writes each from one region show 0 stale answers on Tigris, AWS S3 and R2 (`just test-provider`). `just consistency-probe-tigris 10000` is the long run; none for R2 |
| U6 | Behaviour on versioned buckets and buckets with lifecycle rules, and a soak on AWS S3 and R2 | Never run. The provider checks of 2026-11-24 ran on unversioned buckets |
| U7 | Whether inode reuse can make a replaced database look like the same file | Not examined |
| U8 | The wall-clock lag bound when healthy | G4. No test asserts one |

### Accepted limits

| # | Limit | Why accepted |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | No file certifies its predecessor. Every file is written without a running page checksum, as Go's default is, so a chain of individually valid files with wrong content restores without error (REST-9) | Decision D2 in `docs/PLAN.md`: Go's compactor rejects tracked checksums, and Go must stay a fallback |
| L2 | The LTX file checksum covers decompressed pages, not the compressed bytes. Two different byte strings can decode to the same pages and both verify | Go's format. The upload token carries a digest of the raw bytes for the engine's own files |
| L3 | The engine keeps one file of its own across restarts, the takeover record (`<meta>/lsr-takeover`, BOOT-30), besides the staged files | The root of D1, D3, D9 |
| L4 | A lying disk makes the replica run ahead of the database | No software fix. Detected only while the regrown WAL differs (BOOT-9) |
| L5 | The simulator and the engine can share a wrong belief about SQLite or the disk | Reduced by the history judge, real-SQLite crash cycles and LazyFS; not removed |

### What the evidence does not establish

- Recall. In the one measurement, the simulator alone found 7 of 22 planted bugs with no test written for them; 13 were masked in that run (the description of #86).
- The crash law is a model. It has not been compared with what real disks leave.
- Nobody outside the project has reviewed the code or this document.

---

## 7. How this document stays true

Part of the rows-as-data scheme is built. The tables of section 3 are rendered from `docs/behavior.toml`, one `[[row]]` per row with every cell verbatim, into this file's template (`docs/behavior/template.md`, `just behavior-doc`), and `just check` fails when this file differs from what they render (`crates/compat/tests/compat/behavior.rs`). It also checks every evidence cell: each citation has the form `kind::`\`name\``, each `plant::` names a ledger entry, each `oracle::` and `judge::` a variant that exists, each test citation a test that `cargo nextest list` knows (or a simulator scenario), a row with no test carries a no-test mark, and an In review cell starts with a PR number, a branch tag or `None`. Turning it on corrected five citations (three helper functions cited as tests, one simulator test cited by the core test's name, one module path), two rows whose notes sat in the In review column, and BOOT-17, which cited a plant and no test. A `plant::` citation must be backed: the row also cites a test the ledger entry runs (same package, a test filter that matches), and `just evidence-fresh` requires one such test not to be ignored, since the nightly ledger run (`just planted-bugs`) runs no ignored test. That is what makes a cited plant mean "this row's test kills a bug of this class". The marks themselves are still typed by a person reading tests: the rule they follow is below.

### 7.1 The rule used for the marks in this version

- **Detected by** lists every cited test. A row has D only if at least one is listed.
- **Recovered by** lists only cited tests from the simulator, the Go compat harness, the history rig or the real-SQLite crash tests, because only those judge a restore on content. A core, cli, local, replica, config or store unit test never earns R. Where the test's circumstance is narrower than the row's (one injected error for "credentials revoked"), R is earned for the narrow case and the Notes say what is not shown.
- **Survived by** lists a test only when its fault is still in place at the judged restore, by reading its operation list. Five rows. In four the engine has stopped staging, so S means "what was uploaded before still restores". In one (UP-2) a 5% rate fault is active. No row has S under a store that is down, revoked credentials, a full disk, a second writer's files, or an object that stays rotten.
- A restore judged against a model that adopted the damage earns neither S nor R (RUN-12). Since C8 was fixed, such a restore fails the run (CHK-6).
- **No-test mark** is filled when none of the three is, or for the half of a row that has no test.

### 7.2 How the tables map to the data file

Every table in section 3 has the same twelve columns and converts one row to one `[[row]]` table:

| Column | TOML key | Form |
|---|---|---|
| ID | `id` | Stable. Never renumbered; a removed row leaves a gap |
| Circumstance and how it is detected | `circumstance` | Prose. Backticked enum variants in it become `variants` |
| Response | `response` | Starts with one of: continue, snapshot, retry, degraded, stop, refuse |
| Signal | `signal` | Backticked names from the health-signal table |
| Data at risk, and the bound | `at_risk` | Prose |
| Recovery | `recovery` | Starts with: Automatic, Operator, n/a, or **unknown** |
| Detected by | `evidence.detected` | A list of `crate::`\`test_name\`, then after a semicolon `oracle::`, `judge::` and `plant::` entries, which go to `evidence.checks` |
| Survived by | `evidence.survived` | A list of `crate::`\`test_name\` |
| Recovered by | `evidence.recovered` | A list of `crate::`\`test_name\` |
| No-test mark | `evidence.none` | One of the six marks, with an optional scope before a colon |
| Notes | `notes` | Prose. Never evidence |
| In review | `in_review` | PR numbers or branch tags |

The index tables for `Fault`, `PersistentCause`, `SnapshotCause` and `StopCause` keep their `<!-- enum: X -->` markers and their first-column variant names.

### 7.3 What the scheme must check, including the second review's corrections

1. **Closed worlds.** Each closed enum's variants, through a wildcard-free match, equal the `variants` across rows: `Fault`, `PersistentCause`, `SnapshotCause`, `StopCause`, `BugSite`, `RecoverEvent`, `PlanOutcome`, `RetentionHold`, `StartError`, `lsr_sqlite::Refusal`, `Stopped`, `Gone`, `RestoreError`, the CLI's `Exit`, `S3Code`. The simulator's injectors (`Op`, `MetaOp`, `RemoteOp`, `WalOp`, `EnvFault`, `FaultShape`) likewise against each row's `injector` or `rig: none`.
2. **Marks are derived from what the rigs observed, not typed.** The simulator and the history rig emit one evidence record per judged restore: row id, injector, whether the injector was active at that step, whether it had cleared, which judge ran, the verdict. The gate computes D, S and R from the records of a run. A test cannot earn S unless a restore was judged on content while its injector was active. The three evidence columns then become generated output and stop being input.
3. **Every citation resolves.** Each evidence entry is `package::test`, one per entry, found by `cargo nextest list`. An ignored test counts only with a `runs_in` recipe that a workflow invokes. Every other citation must be a path in the tree, a commit, or a PR or issue number; anything else fails.
4. **Staleness.** The file records `read_at`. The gate fails unless `read_at` is `origin/main`, or no commit since touches a cited symbol or test.
5. **Derived columns equal the code.** `response` equals `policy` and the default `Responses`; `signal` equals `cause_name`, `snapshot_name`, `stop_name`.
6. **Marks have owners.** A no-test mark needs an issue number. An `in_review` tag whose PR merged fails until the row is updated. This version would have failed that check on the tag for #98.
7. **Known wrong is an expected failure.** A known-wrong row names a test that fails on main, or carries "(no failing test)", which the gate counts and prints. Today one row has such a test (RUN-3's restart half) and six rows or half-rows do not.
8. **Every citation can fail (nightly).** Each cited test is linked to a planted bug that it kills. The plant must change the function the row's detection names, or the response path; a patch elsewhere that happens to fail the test does not count.
9. **The cited test reaches the detecting branch (nightly).** Region coverage of the branch, not the function: every test reaches `policy` and `Actor::tick`.
10. **Sections 1 and 6 are generated.** The table at the top of section 1 comes from the data, so a sentence like "three of four have an open violation" cannot go stale by itself. No count over rows is committed: `just behavior-tally` prints one.
11. **A generic harness**, where a row has a simulator injector: run the injector, and assert the observed response, signal and restore outcome equal the row's.

### 7.4 What this still does not do

It does not find the circumstance nobody wrote down. The way to close the world is over the engine's interface to it, which is finite: every syscall it makes on the database, WAL and staging; every S3 operation; the clock; signals; config keys; the pragmas it reads. Cross each with a fixed set of failure modes (error, delay, wrong result, stale result, partial result, duplicate, reorder, lasting). Each cell is a row, an "impossible, because", or a gap. That inventory is not in this version. It is the largest thing missing from this document.

### Changes from version 2

- Read again at 50fb763. G3's violation is closed by #98. G2 has no violation shown on main; what remains is id reuse across a restart, moved under G1 with its ignored expected-fail test.
- Every citation is now a repository path, a test, a commit, or a PR or issue number. Five scratchpad reports are no longer cited and the figures that depended on them are cut.
- Evidence split into one column per mark, by the rule in 7.1. Corrected: RUN-12 (S removed), RUN-7, UP-1, CKPT-1, RUN-11 (R or S removed), UP-2 (S, not R), RUN-4 (R from the healed scenario only), MAINT-3, CFG-7, CFG-4; UP-4, UP-5, RUN-14, RUN-15, UP-12, UP-10 and BOOT-14 gain R for the narrow case their test shows, so the same test shape earns the same mark everywhere.
- G0 (a restore succeeds and is not older than health reported) and G7 (health is not clean while replication is halted) added, with UP-11, MAINT-11, D2, D12 and N1 counted against them. G1 no longer treats a lagging listing or a rolled-back bucket as an assumption.
- C8 added. U4 restated. WORLD-32 to WORLD-40 added. Line numbers removed.

### Changes for #104

- RUN-3 rewritten: a failed WAL fsync is the persistent cause `WalSyncFailed`, degraded by default, and the engine pays for it with a TRUNCATE and a snapshot; D R. Its restart half stays known wrong (D2), now with two ignored expected-fail tests. BOOT-21's in-review tag removed.
- D12 closed: every retried fault stalls at the 8th failure in a row. RUN-13, RUN-14, CKPT-3, WORLD-16, the health-signal table, the fault index and the persistent-cause, snapshot-cause and stop-cause tables updated. G7 no longer counts D12.
